

===== PAGE: ability_to_download_imported_files.html | Ability to Download Imported Files =====
## Ability to Download Imported Files
## Description
 You now can download files imported with MEDS Ingestor.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-787019 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with permission to manage import jobs or import data. To download files for a given data type, your platform must include the respective "Import..." or "Manage..." building block. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 With the ability to download imported files, you can review imported files and perform maintenance tasks such as troubleshooting files that fail to import. 
## User Impact
 From the Job Files screen > Job Configurations panel > Input Files section, you can download any files imported during a given job run. You can download both newly uploaded files and all historical files uploaded before this release.
 Image of a job's Job Configuration screen with file download links. 
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides new and updated content to support this enhancement: 
- Track Progress for Clinical Data Import Jobs 
- Track Progress for CTMS Jobs 
- Track Progress for Patients Data Import Jobs 
- Track Progress for Study Design Metadata Jobs 
- Track Progress for Study Master Data Jobs 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: about_ingestor.html | About Ingestor =====
## About Ingestor
 Ingestor is a software-as-a-service (SaaS),cloud-based ingestion tool that imports data from external data sources directly into the Medidata platform using files such as comma-separated value (.csv) or SAS(.xpt) files.  With Ingestor, you can load non-Rave Clinical Data, non-Rave Clinical Operational Data, Study Master Data, and Clinical Trial Management System (CTMS). Each type of data that Ingestor supports has associated rules and options for how to structure data for successful loading.

===== PAGE: add_ftps_locations.html | Add FTPS Locations =====
## Add FTPS Locations
 You can configure export jobs to send a copy of extracted files to a server using FTPS . To do so, you must first add one or more FTPS locations to your client division. After adding one or more FTPS locations, the list of locations is available to other users within your study group.  
 Important : 
- You are responsible for the access policies, file retention policies, and security of your external FTPS clients. Medidata does not manage your clients' configurations. 
- To ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers:
- Update your FTPS clients' permit-list and deny-list to accept dynamic IP addresses to ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers. 
- The FTPS account must have full access to the parent directory specified in the FTPS settings folder path and all subfolders. This includes read, write, update, and delete privileges.
- In Extractor, click Settings to enter the Output Settings screen. 
- Click Add Locations , and enter the following settings:
- 
 Data Transfer Direction 
 Configures the FTPS location to allow data export, data import, or both.
- Export Data  –  Select to create a location for exporting data. Only applicable for Extractor export jobs. 
- Import Data –  Select to create a location for importing data. Only applicable for Ingestor import jobs. 
- 
 Location Type 
 Extractor only supports the FTP over SSH (FTPS) protocol supported by Extractor.
- 
 Host 
 The destination FTPS server's full host name.
- 
 Port 
 The standard FTPS port is 22. 
- 
 Path 
 The relative path from the FTPS root to the destination folder for the connection.
 Important : The path can be case-sensitive depending on your FTPS configuration.
- 
 User Name 
 The username with write access to this target folder. Use connection-specific user names for these write-access connections to the FTPS server.
- 
 Password 
 Specify either the location password. This is a plain text field, with no validation.
- 
 Encryption Mode 
 Select the encryption mode as defined by your FTPS policy
- 
 Name 
 This name appears to a Extractor user when creating an export job. Ensure that this name is meaningful to the other users.
- Click Add Location . 
 Tip : More Actions for Output Locations 
 After creating output locations, you can perform the following actions:
- Test Location – You can test output locations on the Output Locations screen:
- Find the location you created, and click Options > Test Location . 
- Delete   Location – You delete output locations on the Output Locations screen:
- Find the location you created, and click Options > Delete Location .

===== PAGE: add_sftp_locations.html | Add SFTP Locations =====
## Add SFTP Locations
 You can configure export jobs to send a copy of extracted files to a server using a secure file transfer protocol (STFP). To do so, you must first add one or more SFTP locations to your client division. After adding one or more SFTP locations, list of locations is available to any others user within your study group.
 Important : 
- You are responsible for the access policies, file retention policies, and security of your external SFTP clients. Medidata does not manage your clients' configurations. 
- To ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers:
- Update your SFTP clients' permit-list and deny-list to accept dynamic IP addresses to ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers. 
- The SFTP account must have full access to the parent directory specified in the SFTP settings folder path and all subfolders. This includes read, write, update, and delete privileges.
 Important : 
- You are responsible for the access policies, file retention policies, and security of your external SFTP clients. Medidata does not manage your clients' configurations. 
- To ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers:
- Update your SFTP clients' permit-list and deny-list to accept dynamic IP addresses to ensure proper connectivity and compatibility with Medidata's servers. 
- The SFTP account must have full access to the parent directory specified in the SFTP settings folder path and all subfolders. This includes read, write, update, and delete privileges.
- In Extractor, click Settings to enter the Output Settings screen. 
- Click Add Locations , and enter the following settings:
- 
 Data Transfer Direction 
 Configures the SFTP location to allow data export, data import, or both.
- Export Data  –  Select to create a location for exporting data. Only applicable for Extractor export jobs. 
- Import Data –  Select to create a location for importing data. Only applicable for Ingestor import jobs. 
- 
 Location Type 
 Extractor only supports the FTP over SSH (SFTP) protocol supported by Extractor.
- 
 Host 
 The destination SFTP server's full host name.
- 
 Port 
 The standard SFTP port is 22. 
- 
 Path 
 The relative path from the SFTP root to the destination folder for the connection.
 Important : The path can be case-sensitive depending on your SFTP configuration.
- 
 User Name 
 The username with write access to this target folder. Use connection-specific user names for these write access connections to the SFTP server.
- 
 Password or Private Key 
 Depending on your SFTP policy specify either the password, or a private key for the SFTP user. This is a plain text field, with no validation.
- 
 Location Name 
 This name appears to a Extractor user when creating an export job. Ensure that this name is meaningful to the other users.
- Click Add Location . 
 Tip : More Actions for Output Locations 
 After creating output locations, you can perform the following actions:
- Test Location – You can test output locations on the Output Locations screen:
- Find the location you created, and click Options > Test Location . 
- Delete   Location – You delete output locations on the Output Locations screen:
- Find the location you created, and click Options > Delete Location .

===== PAGE: assign_roles_and_permissions.html | Assign Roles and Permissions =====
## Assign Roles and Permissions
 You must use  Medidata Cloud Administration™ to give users relevant building blocks to access Ingestor. B uilding blocks grant users various permissions to create and manage new types of import jobs, and to import types of data.     To access and use Ingestor, users'platform role  must include the appropriate building blocks.  For a detailed list of applicable building blocks, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List . 
## Configure Roles
 You must configure customer-defined platform roles before you can assign the roles to users. You can either create roles or update roles. For more information on configuring roles in Cloud Administration, see Assign User Roles . 
## Create Role 
 You can create new roles specifically to access Ingestor. 
- Log into  iMedidata.com . 
- Click your study group in the  Study Groups  pane. 
- Click  Manage Roles . 
- Select  Add Roles   >  Add New Role . 
- Complete the  Add a Role  form:
- Name   – Enter role name. 
- Standard Role  – Select any role.
- Ingestor  – Select one or more building blocks. 
- Click  Save . 
## Assign Roles
 You must assign customer-defined platform roles to users for the users to access Ingestor. You can assign users are either the client division  level or the study level.
## Assign Roles at Client Division Level
 For more information on assigning roles at the client division level in Cloud Administration, see  Assign User Access to Study Groups .
- Navigate to a client division in Cloud Administration. 
- Click   Manage People   in the left-hand navigation. 
- Open the   Add Person menu  and click   Add Access . 
- Select the invitation email locale.
 Note :  Information about Email Invitation Locale 
 The invitation menu only sets the locale of invitation emails for users without existing iMedidata accounts. Existing users receive notifications in their current locale.
- Enter user email addresses into the Email field.
You can enter up to 25 email addresses. 
- Select   Client Division . 
- Select any applicable roles from the Roles menu. 
- Click   Assign Access . 
## Assign Roles at Study Level
 For more information on assigning roles in Cloud Administration, see  Assign User Access to Study Environments and Sites .
 Tip : You can also upload a user data file to Cloud Administration to update multiple users. For details, see  Upload Users to a Study .
- Log into iMedidata.com . 
- Click your study in the  Studies  pane. 
- Click  Manage Access . 
- Click  Add Users >  Add Access 
- Type or paste the emails of the users you want to add into the  Emails field.
 Tip : You can enter up to 25 email addresses at once.
- Select the environment you grant access. 
- Select the applicable roles from the  Roles  dropdown. 
- Click Assign Access .

===== PAGE: auto-refresh_for_import_job_summary.html | Auto-Refresh for Import Job Summary =====
## Auto-Refresh for Import Job Summary
## Description
 When reviewing the import job summary for any import job, the Import Summary panel now automatically refreshes every 30 seconds.   
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-678686 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 With the Job Files page's auto-refresh capability, you can see up-to-date information about your processing files without needing to manually refresh the list. 
## User Impact
 While viewing an import job's Import Summary panel, the reported data refreshes every 30 seconds. You can still manually refresh the data by clicking the Refresh Data button on the Job Files page. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: automatic_retries_for_failed_file_uploads.html | Automatic Retries for Failed File Uploads =====
## Automatic Retries for Failed File Uploads
## What Is New?
 Ingestor now automatically attempts to retry file uploads that fail. This occurs for imports done through the Ingestor interface and through SFTP.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-1196223 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All.
## Workflow Process
 No impact to the workflow process.
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 By reattempting file upload, Ingestor saves you direct intervention when files fail to upload due to minor issues.
## Where Do I See the Change?
 No changes to the user interface.
## Where Do I Learn More?
 There are no additional relevant resources.
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: compressed_file_support_for_import_jobs.html | Compressed File Support for Import Jobs =====
## Compressed File Support for Import Jobs
## Description
 You can now load compressed files when importing data. When uploading ZIP files, Ingestor extracts the files and processes them normally. Accepted compressed file types include: .zip, .rar, and .7z.  
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-908089, MCC-908092, MCC-908096, MCC-908110, MCC-908111, MCC-930760, MCC-930776, MCC-937469, MCC-941819, MCC-941824, MCC-943680 
## Affected Roles
 All platform roles that can import files. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 You can upload files containing large data files or bundle files together, such as Study Design Metadata files. 
## User Impact
 You can use ZIP files when uploading files to import jobs with the Ingestor interface, SFTP, or API.
- The constituent files must adhere to the data type's file standards.  
- Compressed files must contain compatible constituent files. Ingestor skips incompatible files during data processing. 
- For external EDC jobs, if the compressed file has multiple file types, then to ensure successful loading, the file types must have the same column headers. When loading clinical data, ensure that you configure the Fields SDM file correctly. XPT files cannot contain more than eight characters in their column headers. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support the added compressed file support: 
- Import Data 
- Track Job Progress 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: configure_external_file_locations_for_imports.html | Configure External File Locations for Imports =====
## Configure External File Locations for Imports
 When creating import jobs, you can configure the job to retrieve files from an external server, such as SFTP and FTPS. You must configure external file locations with Data Connect. After adding one or more locations, the list of locations is available to Ingestor and Extractor users in your client division. 
 You can configure external locations in one of the following ways:
- Add FTPS Locations 
- Add SFTP Locations 
 Important : To configure the list of external file locations, you must use Data Connect. To access Data Connect and use its external location configuration capability, your platform role must include the Extractor Admin building block. For details, see Grant Access to Extractor  and Data Connect Roles and Capabilities .

===== PAGE: content_flow_for_edc_data.html | Content Flow for EDC Data =====
## Content Flow for EDC Data
 When you use Ingestor to import external EDC data , the Medidata platform processes that data for use in other  applications such as CTMS and Medidata Detect. The flowchart below shows the content lifecycle. 
 MEDSINGEST_FlowChart_Master_EDC-Content-Flow_ENG
- As you enter data into Cloud Administration and Rave , the Medidata platform loads that data. 
- You use Ingestor to create a Study Design Metadata (SDM) import job and import SDM data files. The SDM data files include concept tags standard to SDM , and may include External EDC Standard Concepts . 
- Medidata Designer processes the SDM files, creates or updates a protocol, and loads that data onto the Medidata platform. 
- You use Ingestor to create an external EDC import job and import clinical data and clinical operational data. Ingestor processes the data through the Medidata Data Fabric. 
- The Medidata Data Fabric processes the data into various systems and applications that consume platform data, such as CTMS , Medidata Detect , and Platform Site Payments . 
- You can use imported data in the consuming applications after the applications' next refresh.

===== PAGE: content_flow_for_study_master_data_and_ctms_data.html | Content Flow for Study Master Data and CTMS Data =====
## Content Flow for Study Master Data and CTMS Data
 When you use Ingestor to import  Study Master Data or CTMS Data, the data appears in other  applications such as Cloud Administration and Issue Management, and later in the Medidata Data Fabric . The flowchart below shows the content lifecycle. 
 MEDSINGEST_WorkFlow_Master_Content-Flow-MasterData-CTMSData_ENG
- For Study Master Data files: 
- You use Ingestor to create a Study Master Data import job and import Study Master Data files .  The Study Master Data files can include data such as client divisions and team membership. For details, see  Study Master Data File Standards . 
- After Ingestor processes the files, the updated or created data appears in Cloud Administration in their respective categories. Client division data updates Cloud Administration's Master Data. Study environment sites and team membership data updates Cloud Administration's study administration data. You can use imported data in the consuming applications after the applications' next refresh. 
- For CTMS data files:
- You use Ingestor to create a CTMS Data import job and import CTMS data files .  The CTMS files can include data such as enrollment planning, issues, and tasks. For details, see  Medidata CTMS Data File Standards . 
- After Ingestor processes the files, the updated or created data appears in the respective CTMS application. You can find enrollment planning data in Study Management . You can find issues and actions data in Issue Management . You can find tasks data in Task Management . You can use imported data in the consuming applications after the applications' next refresh. 
- The Medidata Data Fabric loads data into various systems and applications that consume Data Fabric data, such as Extractor .

===== PAGE: create_import_jobs.html | Create Import Jobs =====
## Create Import Jobs
 With Ingestor, you can specify the type and destination of the data to import . You must create an import job before you can import data. The steps required to create an import job differ depending on which data type you intend to import. You can create jobs to import the following types of data:
- Study Design Metadata (SDM)  – You can a utomatically create data integration and protocol configurations from predefined comma-separated value (CSV) files. 
- Clinical Data from EDC Sources – You can import clinical trial data from external sources. 
- Patients Data from Electronic Data Capture (EDC) Sources – You can activate or inactivate patients.  
- Study Master Data – You can import data corresponding to various Cloud Administration fields. 
- Clinical Trial Management System (CTMS) Data – You can import data corresponding to the appropriate fields in the respective Medidata CTMS capability. 
 For a walkthrough about loading clinical data for Medidata CTMS, see  Import Non-Rave Data for Medidata CTMS . 
## Create Job
- Log into iMedidata.com . 
- Navigate to Ingestor through your assigned client division.
- Click New Import Job .
- From the Data Type menu, select a data type. 
- Click  Continue . 
- As necessary, select a study that the import job applies to from the Study menu.
 Tip : You can enter the study name to filter the study list.
- Click Continue . 
- Enter a Job Name. 
- If necessary, click the Email Notifications toggle. 
- Click  Continue . 
- If necessary, click the SFTP settings toggle, and enter your SFTP settings:
- SFTP Location – Select from the menu.
 Note : To configure the list of SFTP locations, you must use Extractor. For details on setting up an sFTP location, see  Configure External File Locations for Imports . 
- Folder Location to Retrieve Files – If necessary, enter the extension to the path described in the SFTP Location to import files from.  When left blank, Ingestor uses the SFTP location's root folder. 
- Global Input Folder Toggle – Sets the selected SFTP location as a global import folder. When multiple import jobs use the same SFTP Input File Path, Ingestor can identify the appropriate jobs and transfer the corresponding files from the file names.
 Note : If you want to use an SFTP location as a global location in Ingestor, then you must configure the SFTP location for global use on the SFTP location configuration screen. You cannot mark an SFTP location as global in Ingestor if it is not set as global on the SFTP location configuration screen. 
- Folder Location for Output Status Files  –  If necessary, enter the extension to the path described in the SFTP Location to save process files to.  When left blank, Ingestor uses the SFTP location's root folder. 
- Click  Continue . 
- Review the job's summary details, and click Create Import Job .
 The created import job appears on Ingestor's Jobs List screen. 
## Next Step
 Import Data

===== PAGE: create_import_jobs_in_meds_ingestor.html | Create Import Jobs in MEDS Ingestor =====
## Create Import Jobs in MEDS Ingestor
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor enables the creation of Data Import Jobs. The creation of data import jobs prepares MEDS Ingestor to import supported external data types into MEDS®. You can set import jobs for the specific studies that you configured data integrations for within  Experimental Design .
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-402674, MCC-417821, MCC-445699, MCC-455206, MCC-464283, MCC-469685, MCC-485250, MCC-402678, MCC-417849, MCC-445988, MCC-455637, MCC-464290, MCC-477912, MCC-485264, MCC-402680, MCC-427458, MCC-445989, MCC-456824, MCC-464291, MCC-482585, MCC-402681, MCC-427956, MCC-447328, MCC-457179, MCC-465306, MCC-484355, MCC-410680, MCC-437059, MCC-450125, MCC-458630, MCC-469664, MCC-484631, MCC-414769, MCC-441788, MCC-454635, MCC-464280, MCC-469672, MCC-484659 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 Creating import jobs provides a pipeline to connect your data to MEDS. Because import jobs synchronize with Experimental Design , your external data connects seamlessly to the appropriate studies and environments set in Experimental Design. This connection results in a streamlined data integration process from your data source to Medidata's data repository that saves you time from otherwise manual integrations.
## User Impact
- In Cloud Administration, you can:
- Add the Create Import Job, Import Data, View Import Job, and View Import Summary permissions to your customer-defined platform role which grants access to MEDS Ingestor; and 
- Select MEDS Ingestor from the  Actions dropdown menu from within your assigned study environment. 
- In MEDS Ingestor, you can:
- Create new import jobs; and 
- Import CSV files into existing import jobs from your local drives. 
## Special Considerations
- You must have at least one published protocol in Designer in order to create import jobs. 
- MEDS Ingestor is in a nonfunctional beta release. You can upload and store files with MEDS Ingestor but MEDS Ingestor does not process the files or import the files into MEDS. 
- File import only works with single files.

## Create Import Jobs with Externalized API Endpoints
## Description
 You can use more Medidata's Clinical Cloud API endpoints to create import jobs and import data using MEDS Ingestor resources. The Medidata platform uploads and processes files received through the endpoint. You can also request the status of a given import job, and a list of all import jobs available to you.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-818760, MCC-810588, MCC-818762, MCC-861308, MCC-861271

 All roles with permission to use Clinical Cloud RESTful API Resources. For details, see Get Started with Medidata Platform RESTful API Resources .
## Advantages
 You can programmatically import files using Medidata's API, giving you more options to import data onto the Medidata platform.
## User Impact
 The addition of externalized MEDS Ingestor resources does not affect your use of the MEDS Ingestor user interface.
## Knowledge Space Updates

===== PAGE: ctms_data_import_and_study_master_data_import_now_available.html | CTMS Data Import and Study Master Data Import Now Available =====
## CTMS Data Import and Study Master Data Import Now Available
## Description
 You can now import files of the following data types into MEDS Ingestor:
- Clinical Trial Management System (CTMS) Data, starting with Country Enrollment Planning data. 
- Study Level Master Data, starting with  Client Division Organization with Addresses data. 
 MEDS Ingestor includes the new data types as options in the New Import Job screen. Each data type has its own file requirements and standards.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-748192, MCC-749422, MCC-765978, MCC-749424, MCC-749421, MCC-751376, MCC-779542 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Ingestor.
## Advantages
 The added data types expands your ability to import your data into MEDS® and use the data in other applications, such as MEDS Extractor or MEDS Reporter.
## User Impact
 In Cloud Administration, you can add one or more of the following building blocks:
- Manage CTMS Data – You can create CTMS import jobs. You can also edit the Job Configuration page for CTMS jobs. 
- Import CTMS Data – You can import data using CTMS import jobs. 
- View CTMS Data – You can see CTMS import jobs on the jobs list screen. You can also review the Job Files screen and Import Summary panel for CTMS jobs. 
- Manage Study Master Data – You can create Study Master Data import jobs. You can also edit the Job Configuration page for Study Master Data jobs. 
- Import Study Master Data – You can import data using CTMS import jobs. 
- View Study Master Data – You can see Study Master Data import jobs on the jobs list screen. You can also review the Job Files screen and Import Summary panel for Study Master Data jobs. 
 In MEDS Ingestor, you can:
- Select one of the CTMS Data and the Study Master Data options in the Data Type menu of the New Import Job screen. 
- See CTMS import jobs and Study Master Data import jobs on the Import Jobs screen. 
- Review job files for CTMS and Study Master Data import jobs. 
- Update job configuration details for CTMS and Study Master Data on each job's Job Configuration screen. 
 Imported Study Master Data corresponds to various Cloud Administration fields, and you can confirm the data changes in Cloud Administration.
 Imported CTMS data corresponds to the appropriate fields in the respective CTMS application, such as Study Management. You can confirm the data changes in the respective CTMS application.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: data_import_from_sftp_locations.html | Data Import from SFTP Locations =====
## Data Import from SFTP Locations
## Description
 You can now configure import jobs to retrieve data files from SFTP locations. You must define an input folder path to receive files from and an output folder path to place Ingestor's post-processing files. Every 30 minutes, MEDS Ingestor imports the files from the Input File Path and exports various status files to the output folder path. When applicable, the exported files include processing files such as error lists, reconciliation reports, and success logs.
 In addition, SFTP details appear on each page containing import job details, including the Jobs List page and the Job Configuration page.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-742594, MCC-789541, MCC-791549, MCC-739607, MCC-742610, MCC-739611, MCC-645295, MCC-785967, MCC-739610, MCC-739608, MCC-748772, MCC-766160, MCC-788242, MCC-803365 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 With SFTP import, you have more flexibility in how to import data files onto the Medidata platform. Once you configure an import job to use an SFTP location, you only need to manage the connection, and do not need to search for files or manage file output. Moreover, you can retrieve and place files in a secure location that automatically updates without manual intervention. In addition, you can import large data files more consistently over SFTP instead of a browser since there is no concern from time-out errors or other issues.
## User Impact
 While creating import jobs, you can configure the job's SFTP settings at the SFTP Settings step. After clicking the SFTP toggle, you can set the import job's  SFTP Location, input folder path, and output folder path. After creating the import job, MEDS Ingestor imports the files from the input folder path every 30 minutes. The  various status files appear in the output folder path as they become available.
 You can also edit a job's SFTP settings from the job's Job Configuration page. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides new and updated content to support this feature/enhancement: 
- Create Import Job for CTMS Data  (updated) 
- Create Import Job for Clinical Data from External EDC Sources  (updated) 
- Create Import Job for Study Design Metadata Files  (updated) 
- Create Import Job for Study Master Data  (updated) 
- Review Job Configuration Details (updated) 
- Update Job Configuration Details (updated) 
- Configure External File Locations for Imports (new) 
- Add SFTP Locations (new) 
- Edit SFTP Locations  (new) 
- Test SFTP Locations (new) 
## Special Considerations
 To configure the list of SFTP locations, you must use MEDS Extractor. To access MEDS Extractor and use its SFTP configuration capability, your platform role must include the Extractor Admin building block. For details, see  Grant Access to Extractor  and Extractor Roles and Permissions .

===== PAGE: data_ingestion_flowchart.html | Data Ingestion Flowchart =====
## Data Ingestion Flowchart
 With Ingestor, you can create import jobs to ingest data from various sources into target locations for later use. The flowchart below shows Ingestor's overall data ingestion lifecycle. 
- Prepare data files for importing into Ingestor. The data files must conform to Ingestor's data file standards. For details, see  Import File Standards . 
- In Ingestor:
- Create import jobs  for each data type you want to ingest. 
- Load data files  with the corresponding import job type.
- Ingestor processes the data files. Depending on the data type, sends the data to other services or applications.
- The target services and application create or update data according to the uploaded data files. 
- If there are errors with data processing, the service or application notifies Ingestor. 
- You can  review error details  in Ingestor. 
- Repeat steps 1 and 2 as necessary. 
 Note : 
 When loading clinical data or clinical operational data:
- You must create two import jobs: study design metadata (SDM) and external EDC. You must first create the SDM job and upload the requisite SDM files, then you can create the external EDC job and upload the clinical and clinical operational data files with the external EDC job.  
- SDM import jobs:
- Define the structure of the data to be loaded to the variability of data capture of EDC data. 
- Only require configuration once. When you upload SDM files, Ingestor creates a protocol configuration that you can use for multiple external EDC jobs. You can upload new SDM files to the SDM import job to update the protocol configuration and to capture SDM changes, such as new data fields. For details on how Ingestor processes SDM files, see  Study Design Metadata Import Process . 
- Enable clinical data loading. Ingestor uses the output from successful SDM imports when configuring external EDC import jobs. 
- External EDC data files must match the structure as defined by the imported SDM files. 
- For details on how Ingestor processes external EDC files, see  Content Flow for EDC Data 
 When loading data to Cloud Administration:
- You must create and import data using the Study Master Data type. 
- Data files for Cloud Administration are preset data structures. For file specifications, see  Study Master Data File Standards . 
- For details on how Study Master Data files flow in Ingestor, see  Content Flow for Study Master Data and CTMS Data . 
 When loading data to CTMS:
- You must create and import data using the CTMS data type. 
- Data file for CTMS have preset data structures. For file specifications, see  Medidata CTMS Data File Standards . 
- Study Master data may be necessary to enable CTMS data import. 
- For details on how CTMS data files flow in Ingestor, see  Content Flow for Study Master Data and CTMS Data .

===== PAGE: data_loading_process.html | Data Loading Process =====
## Data Loading Process
 Once the import process begins, an uploaded file goes through several stages of loading. Below is an illustration on all the possible statuses an imported file can be in. 
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
 draw.io 
 Source page access restriction: Click the link below to check if the page is accessible. 
 /display/MEDSINGESTsrc/._draw.io_dataProcessDiagram+v2022.4.x 
 Order
 Status
 Status Description
 User Action
 | 1 
 | Transfer in Progress 
 | 
 The Medidata platform is transferring the file from an external SFTP folder to Medidata’s S3 bucket. 
 Note : This is only applicable when uploading a file using an external SFTP location. 
 | None. 
 | 2 
 | Transfer Failed 
 | 
 The file transfer from the external SFTP location to the Medidata S3 bucket failed. 
 Note : This is only applicable when uploading a file using an external SFTP location. 
 | Check Ingestor’s Job Notifications screen for failure details. 
 | 3 
 | Upload in Progress 
 | The Medidata platform uploading the file to Medidata’s S3 bucket.  
 | None. 
 | 4 
 | Upload Failed 
 | The upload to Medidata’s S3 bucket failed. 
 | Check the error message that appears in the View panel of the Job Details screen . 
 | 5 
 | Waiting to Process 
 | The file is waiting for the processor that loads data onto the Medidata S3 bucket to pick it up. 
 | None 
 | 6 
 | Import in Progress 
 | The processor picked up the file from the queue and is loading it. 
 | None 
 | 7 
 | Import Failed 
 | The Medidata platform failed to start loading from the uploaded queue. 
 | Please contact the Medidata Helpdesk. 
 | 8 
 | Data Validation In Progress 
 | The file is loading, and the Medidata platform is running data validation.  
 When loading external EDC data, the Medidata platform checks file specification against the Study Design Metadata (SDM) definition during this step. 
 | None.
 | 9 
 | Data Validation Failed 
 | The file has failed data validation checks, and the Medidata platform did not load any data.  
 | Check the error file that appears in the View panel of the Job Details screen . 
 | 10 
 | Data Processing In Progress 
 | The file passed data validation is, and the Medidata platform is processing the file’s data. The platform checks each record and attempts to load all possible records. 
 | None 
 | 11 
 | Data Processing Retry in Progress 
 | The Medidata platform retries to load unprocessed rows. For each row, the platform performs up to 14 attempts by default. After the 14 attempts, data processing for that row counts as a failure. 
 | N/A 
 | 12 
 | Data Processing Failed 
 | All records failed to load.  
 | Check the error file that appears in the View panel of the Job Details screen . 
 | 13 
 | Completed Successfully 
 | All records loaded successfully. 
 | Check the Summary Counts that appear in the View panel of the Job Details screen . 
 A Processing Detail file is also available to download in the panel’s Artifacts section that includes a record-level success log. 
 | 14 
 | Completed with Failed Rows 
 | File loading is partially successful. The Medidata platform loaded one or more records successfully.  
 | Check the Summary Counts that appear in the View panel of the Job Details screen for record-level loading number. 
 A Processing Detail File is also available to download in the panel’s Artifacts section that includes a record-level success log. 
 An Error file or Warning file is also available that includes details on records with errors and warnings, respectively.

===== PAGE: data_loading_with_import_jobs.html | Data Loading with Import Jobs =====
## Data Loading with Import Jobs
 With Ingestor, you create import jobs to enable the loading of external data into the Medidata platform.
## Data Types Compatible with Ingestor
 You can upload the following types of data by uploading the appropriate files. For applicable file requirements for each data type, see 
 Import File Standards . For downloadable starter and reference files, see 
 Starter Import Files .
 Note :  Usage Notes for Compressed Files 
 All import job types support compressed files. Compressed files must contain compatible constituent files. For external EDC jobs, if the compressed file has multiple file types, then to ensure successful loading, the file types must have the same column headers.
 Data Type 
 Supported File Types 
 | 
 Study Design Metadata (SDM) data, including:
- Schedule of Assessments (SOA) 
- Visits 
- Data Sets 
- Fields 
 | 
- Comma-separated Value (CSV) 
- Compressed files (.zip, .rar., .7z) 
 | 
 External EDC clinical sources
 | 
- CSV 
- SAS files(.xpt)
- Compressed files (.zip, .rar., .7z) 
 Note :
- Ingestor only supports the SAS V5 Transport file format and below. 
- XPT files cannot contain more than eight characters in their column headers. 
- When loading clinical data, ensure that you configure the Fields SDM file correctly. 
 | External EDC patients data 
 | 
- Comma-separated Value (CSV) 
- Compressed files (.zip, .rar., .7z) 
 | 
 Clinical Trial Management System (CTMS) data, including:
- Country Enrollment Planning 
- Service Providers 
- Tasks 
- Issues 
- Actions 
- Enrollment Metrics 
 | 
- Comma-separated Value (CSV) 
- Compressed files (.zip, .rar., .7z) 
 | 
 Study Master Data, including:
- Client Division Organization with Addresses 
- Client Division People with Addresses 
- Study Environments Sites with Addresses 
- Team Membership Details 
- Team Member Contact Details 
 | 
- Comma-separated Value (CSV) 
- Compressed files (.zip, .rar., .7z) 
## Identify Method to Load Clinical Data
 Identify a method to load clinical data onto the Medidata platform. You can use one of the following methods:
- Method 1 – Create an import job and load data with Ingestor. 
- Method 2 – Create an import job with Ingestor, and load data with SFTP. 
- Method 3 – Create an import job and load data with API resources.  
## Method 1 - Create an Import Job and Load Data with Ingestor
 You can use Ingestor to create import jobs that accept specific data types and then use the Ingestor interface to load the data from your system.
- Create import jobs for each data type you want to load onto the Medidata platform. 
- Upload prepared files using the Ingestor interface.   
## Method 2 - Create an Import Job with Ingestor and Load Data with SFTP
 You can use Ingestor to create import jobs that accept clinical data from an SFTP folder. Ingestor then loads data from the selected folder at regular intervals.
- Configure SFTP locations for Ingestor use. 
- Create import jobs for each data type, and configure them to use SFTP locations. 
## Method 3 - Create an Import Job and Load Data with API Resources
 You can use Medidata API resources to programmatically create import jobs and load data with those jobs. For details, see  Upload External Data onto the Medidata Platform in the Medidata Platform API publication on the Knowledge Hub..
 Important : You cannot create SDM import jobs with the Medidata API. Instead, you must use the Ingestor to create the import job. Once created, you can use the Medidata API to upload files with the job.

===== PAGE: detailed_import_status.html | Detailed Import Status =====
## Detailed Import Status
## Description
 With this release you can monitor the progress of file imports as they occur in different stages of progression. The file Import progresses through file upload, data validation, and data processing before being completed. 
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-451296, MCC-480113, MCC-496842, MCC-498604, MCC-511236, MCC-523728, MCC-542830 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with MEDS Ingestor permissions.
## Advantages
 You can monitor the progress of individual file import jobs. The uploaded file data populates MEDS.
## User Impact
 The Import Summary provides more information about each stage of the data import process.
 Color-coded bars show the progress of data file upload, import, and validation. Errors at any stage cause the import to fail.
 Ingestor displays the current process stage with appropriate colors and text updates.
 The Review Import Jobs page contains the detailed import summary with row counts. 
 Special Considerations 
- You must have at least one published protocol and Data Integration from Medidata Designer to create import jobs. 
- The study must be active in order to create import jobs. 
- You can only import single files.

===== PAGE: edit_import_jobs_in_meds_ingestor.html | Edit Import Jobs in MEDS Ingestor =====
## Edit Import Jobs in MEDS Ingestor
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor allows you to review and edit active import jobs that you have previously created. You can review the current status of all active data import jobs for a given study across environments. When you publish a new version of the source data integration in  Experimental Design  you can also update its corresponding import jobs in MEDS Ingestor to use the latest version. You can also view detailed import history lists describing the status of your imported data.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-380414, MCC-414770, MCC-441796, MCC-450144, MCC-469677, MCC-410291, MCC-417856, MCC-446528, MCC-451303, MCC-469678, MCC-410685, MCC-427957, MCC-450121, MCC-458272, MCC-470252 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 Reviewing active imports allows you to:
- fine-tune existing import jobs to your specifications; and 
- review the success of imported data so you can take further action, if needed. 
## User Impact
 In MEDS Ingestor, you can select an existing import job to review its contents. While reviewing the contents of an existing import job, you can:
- Review import jobs by previously added environments; 
- Add additional environments to the import job; 
- Update the data integration version of the import job, as defined in Designer; and 
- Import data into the import job. 
## Special Considerations
 MEDS Ingestor is in a nonfunctional beta release. You can upload and store files with MEDS Ingestor but MEDS Ingestor does not process the files or import the files into MEDS.

===== PAGE: email_notifications_now_available.html | Email Notifications Now Available =====
## Email Notifications Now Available
## Description
 You can now configure import jobs to send email notifications on job completion. The notifications inform you if the job completed successfully, successfully with failed rows, or unsuccessfully and includes relevant details. Email notifications deliver for jobs leveraging any import method: through the Ingestor interface, through an external sFTP location, or through Medidata API resources. Email notifications trigger for the following users that create jobs, modify jobs, or import files.
 You can configure email notifications while creating import jobs, on an existing job's Job Configuration screen, or while using API resources. 
 Note : The Medidata platform does not deliver email notifications to inactivated users or users no longer in the job's client division.  
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-1027843, MCC-989337, MCC-1007030, MCC-1008875 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 You can configure jobs to inform stakeholders about a given job's completion status without needing to navigate to the Ingestor interface. 
## User Impact
 Existing jobs have email notifications turned off by default. You can enable job notifications for existing jobs from a given job's Job Configuration screen. Likewise, any jobs created using API resources can use 
## Knowledge Space Updates

- Create Import Jobs 
- Update Job Configuration Details

 All users

 No impact to the workflow process.

 Windows-based SFTP servers fail to process files since Ingestor does not automatically create the output folders. With the update, Ingestor now creates output folders for Windows-based SFTP servers.

===== PAGE: ensure_unique_upload_file_names.html | Ensure Unique Upload File Names =====
## Ensure Unique Upload File Names
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor 2020.2.1 requires that any uploaded file to have a unique file name for a given study and environment. If the file is not unique, an error message prevents the file upload starting.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-686689 
## Affected Roles
 All roles   with the Data Import   permission.
## Advantages
 This new unique filename requirement ensures easy traceability between the import job and the data files imported.
## User Impact
 The file name of the import data must be unique for the study and environment of the import job.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: external_edc_file_support.html | External EDC File Support =====
## External EDC File Support
## Description
 You can import data from external EDC systems into MEDS Ingestor.  The following external  data sets  are available for import in the comma-separated value (CSV) format: 
- Disposition Status (DS) 
- Adverse Event (AE) 
- Subject Visits (SV)  
 External EDC file support is only available to internal Medidata staff as of MEDS Ingestor 2020.1.0.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-568905 
## Affected Roles
 All customer-defined platform roles with access to MEDS Ingestor. 
## Advantages
 Import external EDC files into MEDS. Once imported, produce consolidated reports using  MEDS Reporter 
## User Impact
 You can import CSV files from external EDC sources.  CSV files for import must have filenames starting with the data set type corresponding to the data in the file followed by a dash:
- DS- for Disposition Status data. 
- AE-  for Adverse Event data. 
- SV-  for Subject Visit data. 
 Important . These are the only MEDS Ingestor currently only supports the Disposition Status, Adverse Event, and Subject Visit data set types  for external EDC imports.
## Special Considerations
- External EDC file support is only available to internal Medidata staff as of MEDS Ingestor 2020.1.0 .

===== PAGE: external_edc_standard_concepts.html | External EDC Standard Concepts =====
## External EDC Standard Concepts
 Data integrations used in Ingestor use   Medidata Standard Concepts to tie together descriptive and process metadata. This metadata provides semantic meaning to inform external EDC data processing in the Medidata platform. 
## Medidata Standard Data Set Concepts
 Medidata defines the following standard data set concepts. Each of these Medidata-defined data set concepts has a fixed collection of fields. The required fields for each data set concept appear in the Complete OIDs List with their OIDs.
 Data Set Name  
 Data Set CONCEPT OID 
 Definition 
 | Laboratory 
 | LB 
 | Supports the transfer of third-party Lab data. 
 | Adverse Events 
 | AE 
 | Supports the transfer of Adverse Events from third-party EDC systems. 
 | Disposition 
 | DS 
 | Supports the transfer of Subject Status/Milestones from third-party EDC systems. 
 | Subject Visits 
 | SV 
 | Supports the transfer of Subject Visits from third-party EDC systems. 
 | Electrocardiogram 
 | EG 
 | Supports the transfer of third-party electrocardiogram (ECG) data. 
 | Questionnaires 
 | QS 
 | Supports the transfer of questionnaires (QS) data from third-party sources. 
 | Protocol Deviations 
 | DV 
 | Supports the transfer of protocol deviations from third-party sources. 
 | Source Data Verification 
 | SDV 
 | Supports the transfer of Form level source data verification (SDV) data from third-party EDC sources. 
 | Queries 
 | QUERIES 
 | Supports the transfer of atomic Queries from third-party EDC sources. 
## Customer-Defined Data Sets
 You can define your own data sets for upload into Ingestor. Customer-defined data sets must include all the  Medidata Master Data Fields in addition to the data fields you want to import.
## Allowed Data Types
 You can use any of the following field data types in any data set.
 Text or Char 
 These are plain text fields of a defined maximum size.
 Float 
 Float fields are numeric fields that can include any real number. Fields of type float may have a maximal precision specified.
 Integer 
 Integer fields are numeric fields that can contain whole numbers. Depending on the field, there may be a range limit applied to the value.
 Date or Datetime 
 These fields contain date and datetime data. There are a limited range of allowed formats for dates and datetimes understood by Ingestor.
 Date Formats

===== PAGE: externalized_api_endpoints_for_meds_ingestor.html | Externalized API Endpoints for MEDS Ingestor =====
## Externalized API Endpoints for MEDS Ingestor
## Description
 You can use Medidata's Clinical Cloud API endpoints to import data using MEDS Ingestor resources. The Medidata platform uploads and processes files received through the endpoint. You can also request the status of a given file import process, or processing job, as well as the status of the transferred files.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-810580, MCC-810581, MCC-810583, MCC-818763, MCC-818764, MCC-830977, MCC-840159 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with permission to use Clinical Cloud RESTful API Resources. For details, see Get Started with Medidata Platform RESTful API Resources .
## Advantages
 You can programmatically import files using Medidata's API, giving you more options to import data onto the Medidata platform.
## User Impact
 The addition of externalized MEDS Ingestor resources does not affect your use of the MEDS Ingestor user interface.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None. You can find details on the Clinical Cloud API Changelog .  
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: ftps_location_support_for_import_jobs.html | FTPS Location Support for Import Jobs =====
## FTPS Location Support for Import Jobs
## What Is New?
 You can now configure import jobs to use FTPS locations that you add in Extractor.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-1091513, MCC-1060480, MCC-1105067 
## What Is the Impact?
## Users/Roles
 All   platform roles   with permission to configure FTPS locations and configure import jobs. For details, see Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Workflow Process
 While configuring an import job, you can set the job to import from an FTPS location similar to how you set SFTP locations.
## Other Applications
 No impact to other applications.
## How Do I Benefit?
 If you use Medidata-hosted or self-hosted FTPS locations, you can now also use the same locations for import jobs. 
## Where Do I See the Change?
 When creating or editing a job, you can configure it to use the FTPS location. 
## Where Do I Learn More?
 Use the following links to find new and updated content in support of this change:
- Configure External File Locations for Imports (updated) 
- Add FTPS Locations (new) 
## What Else Should I Know?
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: get_started_with_ingestor.html | Get Started with Ingestor =====
## Get Started with Ingestor
 With Ingestor, you create import jobs to enable the importing of external data into the Medidata platform. Depending on your role, you can also import data with import jobs, and manage import job configurations. To get started with Ingestor, see one of the workflows in  Import Job Administration in Ingestor .
 Note :  To grant users access to Ingestor, see  Assign Roles and Permissions . 
 Tip : If this is your first time using Ingestor and you want a complete walkthrough on uploading external EDC data, see  Import Non-Rave Data for Medidata CTMS

===== PAGE: global_sftp_settings_for_import_jobs.html | Global SFTP Settings for Import Jobs =====
## Global SFTP Settings for Import Jobs
## Description
 While configuring SFTP settings for your import jobs, you can set a single global SFTP folder for all of your import jobs within a client division scheme. When configuring an import job to import from an SFTP location, you can set the job to import from this global SFTP location.
 When multiple import jobs use the same SFTP Input File Path, Ingestor can identify the appropriate jobs and transfer the corresponding files from the file names.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-898652, MCC-898690, MCC-899004, MCC-899017, MCC-908305 
## Affected Roles
 All platform role with permission to configure SFTP locations and configure import jobs. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 Previously, when you configure import jobs to import from an SFTP location, you must use a unique SFTP location for each import jobs. You cannot reuse SFTP locations for multiple jobs. This can make import job creation operationally difficult if you manage multiple studies or have enterprise- or large-scale operations. With the new global SFTP location settings, you can connect multiple jobs to a single SFTP folder and cut down on operational resources required to manage multiple SFTP locations.
## User Impact
 While creating import jobs:
- At the SFTP Settings step, you enable the selected SFTP location as a global import folder. The screen includes the required file path and file naming format.
 Note : If you want to use an SFTP location as a global location in Ingestor, then you must configure the SFTP location for global use on the SFTP location configuration screen. You cannot mark an SFTP location as global in Ingestor if it is not set as global on the SFTP location configuration screen.
- The Supported Data Sets panel now appears in the new Summary step that appears after the SFTP Settings step. The Summary step includes the file type supported by the import job and the import file name expectations. The same details appear for existing jobs when you click on the Job Files screen's Supported Data Sets button. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support the added SFTP configuration options: 
- Quick Start Ingestor - Import Clinical Data from External EDC Sources 
- Quick Start Ingestor - Import CTMS Data Files 
- Quick Start Ingestor - Import Patients Data from External EDC Sources 
- Quick Start Ingestor - Import Study Master Data Files 
- Create Import Job for CTMS Data 
- Create Import Job for Clinical Data from External EDC Sources 
- Create Import Job for Study Design Metadata Files 
- Create Import Job for Study Master Data 
- Create Import Job for Patients Data from External EDC Sources 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: import_data.html | Import Data =====
## Import Data
 After creating import jobs, you can use Ingestor to import data. The steps required to import data differ depending on which data type you intend to import. You can import up to 50 files at once. 
 For a walkthrough about loading clinical data for Medidata CTMS, see  Import Non-Rave Data for Medidata CTMS . 
## Accepted File Formats
 Ingestor accepts the following file formats: 
- Comma-separated value (.csv) 
- SAS files(.xpt)
 Note :
- Ingestor only supports the SAS V5 Transport file format and below. 
- SAS file upload is only available for External EDC jobs. 
- Compressed files (.zip, .rar., .7z)
 Note :
- Compressed file upload is available for all import jobs. 
- Compressed files must contain compatible constituent files. 
- For external EDC jobs, if the compressed file has multiple file types, then to ensure successful loading, the file types must have the same column headers. When loading clinical data, ensure that you configure the Fields SDM file correctly. XPT files cannot contain more than eight characters in their column headers. 
## Prerequisites for Importing
 To successfully import data, ensure that:
- You register your study and sites in Cloud Administration. 
- If importing Rave data, your import file only contains subjects that exist in the study. 
- Your data integration contains at least one key field that matches a column header in your import file. 
- Your import file's key fields  uniquely identify all data in your data integration.  Ingestor ignores duplicate rows in your import file. 
- Your import file's file name  is unique to the import job and environment. 
 For details for file specifications, see  Import File Standards . For starter files that you can reference and edit, see Starter Import Files .
 Important : You cannot import data into inactivated studies. 
## Import Data
- Log into iMedidata.com . 
- Navigate to Ingestor through your assigned client division. 
- Identify the import job to import data with, and click the import job's name.
 Tip : You can filter import jobs by different parameters, such as Study, Job Type, and Environment. To filter the import job list, click the Filter icon  for one of the parameters, and select which data to filter by.
- Click  Import New Data .
- From the file browser, select files to import. The import process starts immediately.
 Note : Ingestor processes the imported files depending if the job's import type is cumulative or incremental. Cumulative jobs replace all existing data. Incremental jobs add to or update existing data. Jobs with the incremental import type can   Upsert – add or update existing items – or Delete items. The uploaded file's name determines if Ingestor upserts or deletes items. If the file's name contains "upsert", then upsertion occurs. If the file's name contains "delete", then deletion occurs. 
 You can switch an import job between the cumulative and incremental import types. For details, see Update Job Configuration Details . 
 Tip : You also can drag files onto the file uploader. 
## Next Step
 Track Import Job Progress

===== PAGE: import_file_standards.html | Import File Standards =====
## Import File Standards
 To ensure successful file importing into Ingestor, the imported files must adhere to file name and type standards. The following pages list the file standards and considerations for each data type available for upload with Ingestor: 
- Medidata CTMS Data File Standards 
- External EDC Standard Concepts 
- Study Design Metadata File Standards 
- Study Master Data File Standards 
 For a list of starter files that you can download and edit, see  Starter Import Files .
 Once you prepared the files of a given import job type, you can upload them to the Medidata Data Fabric with the corresponding import job. For details on creating import jobs and the available job types, see Data Loading with Import Jobs .

===== PAGE: import_job_configuration_and_job_files_pages.html | Import Job Configuration and Job Files Pages =====
## Import Job Configuration and Job Files Pages
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor introduces two tabbed panes displaying details of the selected import job: 
- Job Files – Lists all the imported files associated with the executed job. 
- Job Configuration – Displays full details of the selected import job. 
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-616341 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the Data Import permission. 
## Advantages
 The new design provides more detail about the selected import job, and details of every file imported into MEDS Ingestor.
## User Impact
 While reviewing import jobs, you access the two detail panes  by clicking the job name. This simplified user interface provides additional details about the selected import job and files imported by the job.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: import_job_notifications.html | Import Job Notifications =====
## Import Job Notifications
## Description
 You can view notifications about any import jobs that use SFTP locations from a job's Job Notifications page. The Job Notifications page shows any errors and warnings that occurred while the import job tried accessing the SFTP location or file path. A notification icon appears when there are new notifications available. From the Job Notification tab, you can also see the following notification details: 
- Type – The notification's type: Error or Warning 
- Message – The notification's message, including the subject line and details 
- Category – The category that notification pertains to: STFP 
- Last Occurrence Date – The date and time the error or warning last occurred 
- Number of Occurrences – The number of times that the error or warning occurred 
 You can also dismiss notifications from the Job Notifications tab. Notifications refresh every 30 minutes.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-766674, MCC-786581 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 With the Job Notifications page, you can keep track how the import job uses its SFTP location and take action as necessary.
## User Impact
 On MEDS Ingestor's Import Jobs page, you can find a new Alerts column. The Alerts column denotes if an import job has notifications that require review. Clicking an Alert icon takes you to the job's Job Notifications page. 
 On the Files page, you can click the  Job Notifications   tab to open the Job Notifications page. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides new content to support this feature: 
- Review Job Notifications (new) 
## Special Considerations
 To configure the list of SFTP locations, you must use MEDS Extractor. To access MEDS Extractor and use its SFTP configuration capability, your platform role must include the Extractor Admin building block. For details, see  Grant Access to Extractor  and Extractor Roles and Permissions .

## Import Job Wizard Shows Supported Data Sets
## Description
 While creating import jobs, after selecting a job type, the job wizard shows a list of supported data sets and file name expectations for the selected job type.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-845443, MCC-854668, MCC-854667 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the ability to create import jobs. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List 
## Advantages
 With the added data set details, you can determine which import job type serves your data import needs before creating the job. You can also determine the file expectations so you can prepare your import files without issue.
## User Impact
 After selecting an import job type, the Job Settings screen shows a new Supported Data Sets table, which contains details on the import job type's supported data sets. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None. However, for a list of import file standards, see  Import File Standards . 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: import_non-rave_data_for_medidata_ctms.html | Import Non-Rave Data for Medidata CTMS =====
## Import Non-Rave Data for Medidata CTMS
 You can upload external data from non-Rave EDC sources, including Classic Rave, onto the Medidata platform through import jobs. To do so, you must create import jobs and import data files containing specifications that relate to your clinical data. The Medidata platform interprets how to process your clinical data through data integrations, which you configure by importing  Study Design Metadata (SDM) files through SDM import jobs. 
## Medidata CTMS Capability Mapping
 Medidata CTMS requires the following data from non-Rave systems to enable Medidata CTMS functionality: 
- Clinical Data
- Subject Disposition dates, such as enrollment or randomization 
- Adverse Event data 
- Subject Visits 
- Clinical Operational Data
- Source Data Verification (SDV) data 
- Queries data 
 The clinical data and clinical operational data enable the Medidata CTMS capabilities listed in the below table. If requisite data is unavailable, then Medidata CTMS disables the corresponding capability. 
 Clinical Data 
 Clinical Operational Data 
 Medidata CTMS Capabilities
 Subject Disposition
 Adverse Events
 Subject Visits

## Data Loading File Requirements
 Loading data for Medidata CTMS requires two sets of files: SDM files and Data files. The SDM files provide information about the study design, including:
- Visits in the study 
- Data sets  to load 
- Each visit's expected data sets 
- Each data set's expected fields 
 You must describe all data you plan to ingest for the study in the SDM files.
 Notes :
- SD M files must include metadata about any data set you intend to load for a given study. The Medidata platform does not support multiple sets of SDM files per study. 
- SDM is not study environment specific. You can use content in SDM files to load data into any study environment for the study, for example: UAT and Production. 
- The data files must match the content data structures specified in the SDM files. 
## Next Step
 Prepare SDM Files

===== PAGE: improved_content_experience_with_knowledge_hub.html | Medidata Knowledge Hub Provides a Centralized, Transformative Product Content Experience =====
## Medidata Knowledge Hub Provides a Centralized, Transformative Product Content Experience
## Description
 The Medidata Knowledge Hub is a new content experience that replaces the current Knowledge Spaces Help in Ingestor. The Knowledge Hub intends to unify all customer-facing product knowledge into a single self-service site to give you a streamlined, connected, and personalized interface. The Knowledge Hub allows you to fine-tune your search on certain parameters such as your role or what you are looking for related to your product. 
 The Knowledge Hub puts you, the Medidata user, front and center, and empowers you to self-serve through an innovative, best-in-class knowledge experience integrated with all Medidata products. You do not have to sift through multiple sources of information, but rather enjoy a unified experience, seamlessly integrated with your  product.  
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 None. 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 Knowledge Hub benefits include: 
- A user-friendly design that provides content from all sources in one site. 
- A faceted search that uses product features to refine your results using machine learning and a built-in taxonomy, which is global and consistent. 
- A personalized user experience that lets you define and save your own subset of the content. 
- A fully integrated on-screen user assistance, which gives you quicker time to resolution in a self-service support model.  
- A cross-platform workflow that helps you understand and go through the entire Knowledge Hub. 
## User Impact
 You access the new Knowledge Hub for Ingestor when you click:
- Help > Knowledge Space Home 
- Show More... on applicable interface help guides. 
 When you enter the Knowledge Hub for the first time, you see a series of short tutorials to introduce this new, streamlined experience. 
## Knowledge Updates
 The Knowledge Space contains no new updates. See the new Knowledge Hub at learn.medidata.com . It currently provides the same content as the Knowledge Spaces site and will be expanded with additional content over time.
## Special Considerations
 As of 03 Apr 2023 , the Knowledge Hub will be the single source for product knowledge, and the Knowledge Spaces site will be shut down. Any saved links or bookmarks to pages on the Knowledge Spaces may no longer work. You can either: 
- Update your bookmarks, or 
- Use the new “My Collections” feature in the Knowledge Hub to create your own in-hub bookmarks, which you can organize into collections and share with colleagues.

## Improved Import Summary Status Interface
## Description
- The data row count found on the import job's import summary now accumulates across retries. Previously, the displayed row counts only showed the rows uploaded for the last retry attempt. 
- MEDS Ingestor 2020.2.1 removes the Laboratory data type for import job creation. The External EDC data type  now allows import jobs to import laboratory data.   
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-678535, MCC-689968 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the Data Import permission.
## Advantages
 The simplified user interface provides accurate status information when displaying upload jobs' status information.
## User Impact
- The Laboratory data type no longer appears in the data type menu. 
- The import summary information now includes the results from automatic retries. 
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: improvements_to_import_job_summary.html | Improvements to Import Job Summary =====
## Improvements to Import Job Summary
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor enhances the Import Jobs summary page to improve the workflow with data integrations from Medidata Designer.  The Import Jobs summary shows  that one import job links to one data integration version per environment.  You can mark an import job as locked or complete, to finalize or obsolete versions of import jobs.  An alert also appears when a newer version of a data integration exists for an import job. 
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-485909, MCC-499267, MCC-508643, MCC-524116, MCC-480119, MCC-464363, MCC-497732, MCC-497736, MCC-480490, MCC-490653, MCC-490654, MCC-441817, MCC-495006, MCC-502169, MCC-499395, MCC-500406, MCC-513132 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Ingestor. 
## Advantages
 You can:
- use different data integrations with multiple different environments. This allows you to test a data integration in an auxiliary environment without impacting production import jobs. However, you cannot use the same data integration across multiple import jobs. 
- mark jobs as Complete by selecting Complete from . Completing a job ensures it is not available for additional file uploads. 
- mark jobs as Locked by selecting Locked from . Locking a job indicates it is ready for operational use by preventing the assigned data integration version from being changed. 
## User Impact
 In Medidata  Cloud Administration y ou can a dd the Perform Admin Actions permission to your customer-defined platform role  which enables the option to mark jobs as Active . Marking a job as active removes the locked state allowing the assigned data integration to be changed. 
## Special Considerations
 Only users with the Perform Admin Actions permission can mark complete import jobs Active.

===== PAGE: incremental_loading_for_external_edc_data_import.html | Incremental Loading for External EDC Data Import =====
## Incremental Loading for External EDC Data Import
## Description
 You can create external EDC import jobs or update existing external EDC import jobs with the Incremental import type. Import jobs with the incremental import type can Upsert – add or update existing items – or Delete items. The uploaded file's name determines if MEDS Ingestor upserts or deletes items. If the file's name contains "upsert", then upsertion occurs. If the file's name contains "delete", then deletion occurs. 
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-751385, MCC-794826, MCC-794827, MCC-804223, MCC-810569, MCC-810571, MCC-810574, MCC-828328, MCC-831291, MCC-811152, MCC-814517, MCC-820388 
## Affected Roles
 All platform roles with either the Import External EDC Data or the Manage External EDC Data building blocks. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 With incremental data loading, you can refresh subsets of your imported data without needing to upload large files containing largely unchanged data. 
## User Impact
 While creating external EDC import jobs, you can select the Incremental import type during the Job Settings step.
 While reviewing an external EDC import job's Job Configuration screen > Import Settings section, you can toggle the job's Import Type between Cumulative and Incremental.
 While uploading external EDC files, each file's file name determines how MEDS Ingestor processes the data file. Include "Upsert" in the file name to create or update records. Include "Delete" in the file name to delete records."
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support this feature: 
- Import Clinical Data from External EDC Sources 
- Track Progress for Clinical Data Import Jobs 
- Review Job Configuration Details 
- Update Job Configuration Details 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: ingestor.html | Ingestor =====
## Ingestor
 Ingestor is a software-as-a-service (SaaS),cloud-based ingestion tool that imports data from external data sources directly into the Medidata platform using files such as comma-separated value (.csv) or SAS(.xpt) files.  With Ingestor, you can load non-Rave Clinical Data, non-Rave Clinical Operational Data, Study Master Data, and Clinical Trial Management System (CTMS). Each type of data that Ingestor supports has associated rules and options for how to structure data for successful loading. 
 Tip : For details about receiving digests on this or other pages, including the release newsletter, see Watch Topics for Updates .

## Ingestor Released Features Index
 This article lists the features and enhancements of Ingestor    as an exportable table with the following highlights: 
- Feature Article – The article link. Click to go to the article's details. 
- Product – The product's name. 
- Version – The product version the update pertains to. 
- Change Type – Feature, enhancement, or technical change. 
- Functionality or Module – The specific functionality area or category the update pertains to. 
- Summary of Changes – A summary of what changes the feature, enhancement, or technical changes introduced. 
 Tip : To learn more about when a capability became available, see the releases history .
 Important :
- The table details appear only in English. Click an article link and change the locale to view translated versions. 
- Features and enhancements introduced before Ingestor 2023.2.5 do not use the current tabular format and therefore do not appear on this table. To learn more about all features and enhancements, see the releases history .

 | 
 The release notes include several formatting updates to address reported challenges and issues with the Medidata Release Notes experience. The changes appear across multiple pages for the release notes.
- Tabular Structure – Pages that describe changes, such as this article, now appear in a tabular structure with renamed or added sections.  
- New sections 
- Product – The product the change applies to. 
- Version – The product version that the change applies to. 
- Change Type – The type of change: 
- Feature 
- Enhancement 
- Technical change 
- Functionality or Module – The area of the product that this section affects, such as a particular module within the product or a subsection within the module. 
- Impact to Existing Data or Settings – Any changes that an update introduces to existing data, such as reports or listings; or updates to user settings that need review. 
- User Impact Assessment – A high-level summary of the change's impact. 
- Changed sections 
- "What's New?" is now "Summary of Changes" 
- "How Do I Benefit?" is now "Benefit to Users" 
- "What's the Impact?" is now "Summary of User Impact" 
- "Users/Roles" is now "Impact to Users and Roles" 
- "Workflow Process" is now "Impact to Workflow Processes" 
- "Other Applications" is now "Impact to Other Applications" 
- Where Do I See This Change? is now "Impact on Interface" and appears in the Summary of User Impact section 
- New Released Features Indices – Release notes now include new Released Features Index articles — one for a specific release, and another for the entire product across releases. These articles list all changes relative to the page's scope as a downloadable table. 
- Feature Release Notes Parent Page – For Feature Release Notes, the home page includes new links to other reference articles such as the released features indices and webinar details, where applicable.

===== PAGE: ingestor_releases_history.html | Ingestor Releases History =====
## Ingestor Releases History
 The following lists all  Ingestor releases (up to and including the current release) and the feature and enhancements introduced in each.
 Code Continuity Across Releases
 All Ingestor releases are cumulative. That is, each release or software update builds on the prior release's as appears in the release notes. For example, Software Update 2016.2.1  includes all the release  2016.2.0  code, which in turn is based on the  release  2016.1.0  code and so on. The release notes describe any updates between releases or s oftware updates (urgent or otherwise) .
