===== PAGE: interface_localization_support_for_apac.html | Interface Localization Support for APAC =====
## Interface Localization Support for APAC
## Description
 Ingestor now supports the following APAC locales:
- Chinese 
- Korean 
- Japanese 
 When you select one of these locales in iMedidata and navigate to Ingestor, Ingestor interface strings appear in corresponding language. Affected strings include titles, button names, and tool tips.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-981834 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 Those in the Chinese, Korean, or Japanese locales can use Ingestor in their native language.
## User Impact
 This update affects how Ingestor appears in the affected locales, but does not affect how you use Ingestor.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 None. 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: job_files_page_updates.html | Job Files Page Updates and SDM File Processing Update =====
## Job Files Page Updates and SDM File Processing Update
## Description
 The Job Files screen includes the following new columns:
- Job Report – Opens the Import Summary panel. 
- File Name – Contains a count of the files uploaded in the processing job. For successful jobs, also shows a list of the uploaded files and their respective data set type.
- Data Set Name – Shows the uploaded data file's data set type. 
- Import Method – Shows the processing job's upload method:
- User Upload – Files uploaded using the MEDS Ingestor user interface. 
- SFTP – Files uploaded using SFTP. 
- API – Files uploaded using API endpoints. 
 The Job Files screen also includes the Supported Data Sets button. Clicking Supported Data Sets shows a list of the data set types the import job type supports.
 In addition, you can upload Study Design Metadata (SDM) files with simplified file names. When a file name contains the name of a data set, MEDS Ingestor processes that file as that data set. For example:
- Datasets – Must contain *datasets.csv; for example:  study_design_datasets.csv  or  datasets.csv. 
- Fields – Must contain *fields.csv; for example: study_design_fields.csv or  fields.csv . 
- Schedule of Assessments – Must contain *soa.csv; for example: study_design_soa.csv or  soa.csv . 
- Visits – Must contain *visits.csv; for example:  study_design_vists.csv or  visits.csv. 
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-846413, MCC-866363, MCC-834704, MCC-866309, MCC-871120, MCC-873682 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the ability to see the Job Configurations details. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 With the updated Job Files screen, you can have more comprehensive data about your processing jobs. In addition, the simplified SDM file name restrictions gives you more flexibility in naming your files.
## User Impact
 See Description. In addition, previously created import jobs include the new columns.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support this enhancement: 
- Track Progress for Clinical Data Import Jobs 
- Track Progress for CTMS Jobs 
- Track Progress for Study Design Metadata Jobs 
- Track Progress for Study Master Data Jobs 
- Study Design Metadata File Standards 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: load_clinical_data_files.html | Load Clinical Data Files =====
## Load Clinical Data Files
 After successfully importing SDM files, you can then create external Electronic Data Capture 
 (EDC) import jobs 
 . After creating an external EDC import job, you can import your data with files in CSV format. 
 A fter creating one or more external EDC import jobs, you can import data into the Medidata platform using comma-separated value (CSV) files, SAS files (.XPT), or a compressed file containing such file types. You can import up to 50 files at once. Accepted compressed file types include .zip, .rar, and .7z. 
 After importing external EDC files, you can track the status of your files from the import job's Job Files screen. The Job Files screen shows data related to the files you uploaded. 
## Identify Method to Load Clinical Data
 Identify a method to load clinical data onto the Medidata platform. You can use one of the following methods:
- Method 1 – Create an import job and load data with Ingestor. 
- Method 2 – Create an import job with Ingestor, and load data with SFTP. 
- Method 3 – Create an import job and load data with API resources.  
## Method 1 - Create an Import Job and Load Data with Ingestor
 You can use Ingestor to create import jobs that accept clinical data and then use the Ingestor interface to load the data from your system. 
## Create Import Job
- Click All Jobs to return to the Jobs List screen. 
- Click New Import Job .
- From the Data Type menu, select  External EDC . 
- Click  Continue . 
- From the Study menu, select a study that the import job applies to.
 Tip : You can enter the study name to filter the study list.
- Click  Continue . 
- Select a data integration, and click Continue .
 Note :  The data integration specifies the expected fields and data upload format of the source data. The data uploaded in the SDM import job determines that data integration. 
- Select a study environment, and click Continue . 
- Enter the following, and click Continue :
- Data Integration Version – The version of the data integration based on the uploaded SDM files. 
- Job Name – The job's name. Must be unique. 
- Import Type – The type of data the job imports. Cumulative jobs replace all existing data. Incremental jobs add to or update existing data. Jobs with the incremental import type can   Upsert – add or update existing items – or Delete items. 
- Email  Notifications – Toggle if you want to receive notifications about the job's completion in your email . 
- Click  Continue . 
- Review the job's summary details, and click Create Import Job . 
## Load Data
- From the Job List screen, identify the import job to import data with, and click the import job's name to enter the Job Files screen.
 Tip : You can filter import jobs by different parameters, such as Study, Job Type, and Environment. To filter the import job list, click the Filter icon  for one of the parameters, and select which data to filter by.
- Click  Import New Data .
- From the file browser, select files to import. The import process starts immediately.
 Tip : You also can drag files onto the file uploader. 
## Method 2 - Create an Import Job with Ingestor and Load Data with SFTP
 You can use Ingestor to create import jobs that accept clinical data from an SFTP folder. Ingestor then loads data from the selected folder at regular intervals. 
- Click All Jobs to return to the Jobs List screen. 
- Click New Import Job .
- From the Data Type menu, select  External EDC . 
- Click  Continue . 
- From the Study menu, select a study that the import job applies to.
 Tip : You can enter the study name to filter the study list.
- Click  Continue . 
- Select a data integration, and click Continue .
 Note :  The data integration specifies the expected fields and data upload format of the source data. The data uploaded in the SDM import job determines that data integration. 
- Select a study environment, and click Continue . 
- Enter the following, and click Continue :
- Data Integration Version – The version of the data integration based on the uploaded SDM files. 
- Job Name – The job's name. Must be unique. 
- Import Type – The type of data the job imports. Cumulative jobs replace all existing data. Incremental jobs add to or update existing data. Jobs with the incremental import type can   Upsert – add or update existing items – or Delete items.     
- Click the SFTP settings toggle, and enter your SFTP settings:
- SFTP Location – Select from the menu.
 Note : To configure the list of SFTP locations, you must use Extractor. For details on setting up an sFTP location, see  Configure External File Locations for Imports . 
- Folder Location to Retrieve Files – If necessary, enter the extension to the path described in the SFTP Location to import files from.  When left blank, Ingestor uses the SFTP location's root folder. 
- Global Input Folder Toggle – Sets the selected SFTP location as a global import folder. When multiple import jobs use the same SFTP Input File Path, Ingestor can identify the appropriate jobs and transfer the corresponding files from the file names.
 Note : If you want to use an SFTP location as a global location in Ingestor, then you must configure the SFTP location for global use on the SFTP location configuration screen. You cannot mark an SFTP location as global in Ingestor if it is not set as global on the SFTP location configuration screen. 
- Folder Location for Output Status Files  –  If necessary, enter the extension to the path described in the SFTP Location to save process files to.  When left blank, Ingestor uses the SFTP location's root folder. 
- Click  Continue . 
- Review the job's summary details, and click Create Import Job . 
## Method 3 - Create an Import Job and Load Data with API Resources
 You can use Medidata API resources to programmatically create import jobs for clinical data and load data with those jobs. For details, see Upload External Data onto the Medidata Platform on the Clinical Cloud API Knowledge Space.

===== PAGE: load_study_design_metadata_files.html | Load Study Design Metadata Files =====
## Load Study Design Metadata Files
 You must configure data integrations and protocol configuration by creating an SDM import job and uploading the required set of SDM files. These data integrations and protocol configurations enable you to upload other types of data, including external EDC data.
 After creating an SDM import job, you then import the four required SDM files using comma-separated value (CSV) files. Importing SDM files creates both of:
- A protocol configuration named <study name>Protocol Configuration at version 1. 
- A data integration named External EDC Data Integration at version 1. 
 After importing SDM files, you can track the status of your files from the import job's Job Files screen. The Job Files screen shows data related to the files you uploaded. 
## Create SDM Import Job
- Log into iMedidata.com . 
- Navigate to Ingestor through your assigned client division.
- Click New Import Job .
- From the Data Type menu, select  Study Design Metadata . 
- Click  Continue . 
- From the Study menu, select a study that the import job applies to.
 Tip : You can enter the study name to filter the study list.
- Click Continue . 
- Enter a Job Name. 
- If necessary, click the   Email Notifications toggle. 
- Click  Continue . 
- If necessary, click the SFTP settings toggle, and enter your SFTP settings:
- SFTP Location – Select from the menu.
 Note : To configure the list of SFTP locations, you must use Extractor. For details on setting up an sFTP location, see  Configure External File Locations for Imports . 
- Folder Location to Retrieve Files – If necessary, enter the extension to the path described in the SFTP Location to import files from.  When left blank, Ingestor uses the SFTP location's root folder. 
- Global Input Folder Toggle – Sets the selected SFTP location as a global import folder. When multiple import jobs use the same SFTP Input File Path, Ingestor can identify the appropriate jobs and transfer the corresponding files from the file names.
 Note : If you want to use an SFTP location as a global location in Ingestor, then you must configure the SFTP location for global use on the SFTP location configuration screen. You cannot mark an SFTP location as global in Ingestor if it is not set as global on the SFTP location configuration screen. 
- Folder Location for Output Status Files  –  If necessary, enter the extension to the path described in the SFTP Location to save process files to.  When left blank, Ingestor uses the SFTP location's root folder. 
- Click  Continue . 
- Review the job's summary details, and click Create Import Job .
 The created import job appears on Ingestor's Jobs List screen. 
## Import SDM Data
- From the Jobs List screen, identify the import job to import data with, and click the import job's name.
 Tip : You can filter import jobs by different parameters, such as Study, Job Type, and Environment. To filter the import job list, click the Filter icon  for one of the parameters, and select which data to filter by.
- Click  Import New Data .
- From the file browser, select files to import. The import process starts immediately.
 Important : You must upload all four SDM files simultaneously.
 Tip : You also can drag files onto the file uploader. 
## Next Step
 Load Clinical Data Files

## Manage Job Configurations
 After creating import jobs, you can review and edit job details on each job's Job Configuration screen. The presented details differ depending on the type of import job. You can perform one of the following actions: 
- Review Job Configuration Details 
- Update Job Configuration Details 
- Update Study Design Metadata for Clinical Data Loading

===== PAGE: medidata_ctms_data_file_standards.html | Medidata CTMS Data File Standards =====
## Medidata CTMS Data File Standards
 To ensure the successful import of Medidata CTMS data files to Ingestor, the files must follow specific file name and format conventions. The imported data corresponds to the appropriate fields in the respective Medidata CTMS application, such as Study Management. You can confirm the data changes in the respective Medidata CTMS application.
## General File Considerations
- Uploaded files must be in the comma-separated value (CSV) file format.
- Files containing empty data cause the import process to fail. 
- Any OID identifier referenced in these files must comply with the following rules: 
- Uppercase alphanumeric text only. 
- No spaces. 
- The OID must begin with an alphabetical character. 
- Underscores can appear in an OID, but not as the first character. 
## File Name Standards
 The file names for your imported files must contain a string denoting which data set  the imported file corresponds to.
 Tip : For files you can use as a model and starting point, see  Starter Import Files . 
 Data Set Type 
 Usage 
 Required String in File Name 
 | Country Enrollment Planning 
 | Plan or revise subject enrollment counts at country level in Study Management . 
 | EnrollmentPlanningCountry 
 | Enrollment Metrics 
 | Plan or revise enrollment metrics at a specified context in Study Management . 
 | EnrollmentMetrics 
 | Service Providers 
 | Create service provider details in Study Management.  
 | ServiceProviders 
 | Tasks 
 | Create tasks details in Task Management.  
 | Tasks 
 | Issues 
 | Create issues details in Issue Management. 
 | Issues 
 | Actions 
 | Create actions details in Issue Management. 
 | Actions 
 Note : The required string is case-insensitive, and you can prefix or append alphanumeric characters to the string by separating the added text with underscores. For example, when uploading a file corresponding to the Country Enrollment Planning data set type, the file name must contain "EnrollmentPlanningCountry". Therefore, Ingestor accepts or rejects the following example file names:
- Valid File Names
- Enrollmentplanningcountry_20210604.csv 
- Enrollmentplanningcountry.csv 
- SponsorA_EnrollmentPlanningCountry.csv 
- Invalid File Names 
- CountryEnrollmentPlanning.csv 
## File Structure Standards
 Uploaded files must follow a specific file structure:
- Your files must include all listed column headers, but optional columns can include empty data. 
- Header records must be on the first row of each metadata file, and must the values specified in the below table's Column Header. 
- Headers with noted acceptance values must contain one or more of the listed values.   
- If a String-based value contains a comma in the string, you must enclose the entire value in quotations. For example: “Congo, Democratic Republic of“. 
 Tip : Click image to expand.

 Grouped services share Services Record IDs. You can update or change service records. You can also add or remove services from the group while maintaining the same Service Record ID. You cannot change individual services in the group; once added to the group all services within a service group function as a single entity.

 Must be countries that appear in Cloud Administration.
 If there are multiple countries, you must separate each with a pipe-delimiter: '|'
 Note : Only applicable when Service Area is "Specific Countries or Sites". 
 | Specific Sites 
 | Optional 
 | The sites the used the service. 
 | 
 Must be sites that appear in Cloud Administration.
 If there are multiple countries, you must separate each with a pipe-delimiter: '|'
 Note : Only applicable when Service Area is "Specific Countries or Sites". 
 | Excluded Countries 
 | Optional 
 | Sites excluded from the Service Area.
 | 
 Must be countries that appear in Cloud Administration.
 If there are multiple countries, you must separate each with a pipe-delimiter: '|'
 Note : Only applicable when Service Area is "All Countries, All Sites". 
 | Excluded Sites 
 | Optional 
 | Countries excluded from the Service Area. 
 | 
 Must be sites that appear in Cloud Administration.
 If there are multiple countries, you must separate each with a pipe-delimiter: '|'
 Note : Only applicable when Service Area is "All Countries, All Sites".

===== PAGE: metadata_file_based_creation_of_data_integrations.html | Metadata File Based Creation of Data Integrations =====
## Metadata File Based Creation of Data Integrations
## Description
 You can use MEDS Ingestor to create data integration configurations by importing Study Design Metadata comma-separated value (CSV) files. You can import third-party external data with no additional requirement for Medidata EDC applications.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-616342, MCC-621155, MCC-645856, MCC-645863, MCC-651077, MCC-654375, MCC-657107, MCC-679435, MCC-679743 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with Data Import permissions. 
## Advantages
 MED Ingestor now enables ingestion of third-party non-Medidata EDC data into MEDS without requiring you to be a Medidata EDC customer. 
## User Impact
 You can upload Study Design Metadata (SDM) files  to create data integrations for third-party data. 
## Special Considerations
 As uploading SDM files creates protocols and data integrations, therefore you must also have Medidata Designer permissions. Therefore,  Medidata Designer no longer supports the creation of data integrations for Laboratory data.

===== PAGE: partially_processed_file_reporting_for_study_master_data_files.html | Partially Processed File Reporting for Study Master Data Files =====
## Partially Processed File Reporting for Study Master Data Files
## Description
 When reviewing the import job summary for Study Master Data import jobs, the Import Summary panel >  Data Processing section shows the following record counts: 
- Processed – The number of rows in the imported file that were processed so far. 
- Partially Updated  – The number of rows in the imported file that could only be loaded partially, for example: some columns were successfully loaded and some were not. 
- Error – The number of processed rows with errors. 
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-786572 
## Affected Roles
 All platform roles with one of the following building blocks:
- View Study Master Data
- Import Study Master Data 
- Manage Study Master Data 
## Advantages
 You can track the progress of individual data without waiting for the import job to finish. This can be especially helpful for large data files that require extended periods of time to finish processing.
## User Impact
 You can find the updated record counts while reviewing Study Master Data jobs' Job Files page> Import Summary panel > Data Processing section. This update retroactively affects the import summaries of your previously created jobs as well. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support this enhancement: 
- Track Progress for Study Master Data Jobs  (updated) 
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: patients_data_import_from_external_edc_sources.html | Patients Data Import from External EDC Sources =====
## Patients Data Import from External EDC Sources
## Description
 You can create and import Patient IDs from external EDC sources using import jobs with the  External EDC - Patients data type. You can activate or inactivate a patient.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-861272, MCC-861273, MCC-861271, MCC-866323, MCC-874398, MCC-874863 
## Affected Roles
 All.
## Advantages
 With the added import job type, you can import more of your data on the Medidata platform and use the data for analysis and processing. You can also activate or inactivate patients without uploading files containing clinical data.
## User Impact
 In Cloud Administration, you can now select one of the following building blocks:
- View Eternal EDC Patients Data 
- Manage External EDC Patients Data 
- Import External EDC Patients Data 
 By selecting one of these building blocks, you can interact with External EDC - Patients import jobs in MEDS Ingestor. For details on the permissions that each building block grants, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List . 
 In MEDS Ingestor,
- While creating import jobs, you can select the External EDC - Patients data type. You can create multiple External EDC - Patients import jobs for the same study or study environment. Only users with the  External EDC - Patients building block can create External EDC - Patients import jobs. 
- While reviewing import jobs on the Jobs List screen, you can see import jobs with the External EDC - Patients data type. You can use the Data Type filter to filter by the new data type.  
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides new and updated content to support this feature: 
 Updated
- About MEDS Ingestor 
- MEDS Ingestor Roles and Permissions 
- Import Job Administration in MEDS Ingestor 
- Create Import Jobs 
- Import Data 
- Track Job Progress 
- Starter Import Files 
 New
- Quick Start MEDS Ingestor - Import Patients Data from External EDC Sources 
- Create Import Job for Patients Data from External EDC Sources 
- Import Patients Data 
- Track Progress for Patients Data Import Jobs 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the  Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: preparations_for_future_development.html | Preparations for Future Development =====
## Preparations for Future Development
## Description
 Ingestor includes the following updates to prepare for future Ingestor capabilities:
- Architecture updates:
- Updates to enable the addition of import job types outside the release cycle:
- Creation of the following internal templates: Run Activity, Import Definition, and Import Configuration 
- Removal of internal job creation and processing dependences 
- Standardization of internal creation job processing assets 
- Updates to Populate Platform identifiers in Ingestor database for future development 
- Removal of obsolete columns from internal database and architecture 
- Updates to prepare Ingestor's interface for localization. 
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-1011623, MCC-961194, MCC-1012597, MCC-1012584, MCC-1012614, MCC-1012621, MCC-1006536, MCC-1009027, MCC-1006534, MCC-1006535, MCC-994153, MCC-1031579, MCC-988704, MCC-1013013, MCC-1015212 
## Affected Roles
 None. This restructuring does not affect the way you use Ingestor.
## Advantages
 These updates prepare Ingestor to accept new data types for import jobs and other capabilities. As the Medidata team introduces new data types, you can use those types to import more types of data onto the Medidata platform through Ingestor.  
## User Impact
 None. This restructuring does not affect the way you use Ingestor. This release does not include new data types, and any new data types do not affect your existing import jobs.
## Knowledge Updates
 None. 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: prepare_clinical_data_files.html | Prepare Clinical Data Files =====
## Prepare Clinical Data Files
 Prepare files containing clinical data and clinical operational data to enable Medidata CTMS functionality for non-Rave studies. You can use variable or fixed data structures depending on the data domain you plan to load. 
 Note : If data collection does not match the defined Schedule of Assessment (SOA) file contents:
- Update the SOA as a post-production change to reflect the new pairs of visits and data sets as a post-production change. 
- Ensure the data provider updates the data file and associates data to the pairs of planned visits and data sets. 
- Add a visit – such as UNSCHEDULED – as a post-production change, and provide the data provider instructions for when to associate data to UNSCHEDULED visit. 
## Required Concept OIDs
 Each data set must have fields tagged to the following Study Master Data concepts. Study Master Data Concepts are key to store and manage the clinical data.

## Organize Subject Disposition Data
 Identify a method to organize Subject Disposition data for loading. You can use one of the following methods: 
- Load data with the source data sets exported from an EDC system. 
- Load data using Subject Disposition data sets. 
## Method 1 - Load Data With Source Data Sets from an EDC System
 You can load the Subject Disposition data from source data sets that you export from an EDC system. Clinical data exported from an EDC system do not map to CTMS functionality and are therefore optional. The Medidata Data Fabric only requires fields pertaining to the required concepts to enable CTMS functionality.
- Prepare the Subject Disposition data file from your EDC system, and do one of the following to the extract: 
- Load all the clinical data into the system.
 Note : This option has an increased loading time.
- Exclude the optional clinical data fields to prevent loading clinical data into the system.
 Note : The system produces warning messages for fields skipped during data processing.
- Remove the clinical data fields from source EDC data extracts.
 Note : In the following example, the user loaded every field extracted from the EDC system. When doing so in your case, ensure that you include the correct Data Set and Visit metadata in the Schedule of Assessments SDM file to reflect the study design. 
 STUDYID
 SITEID
 SUBJID
 VISIT
 SCREENING_DATE
 SCREEN_FAILED_DATE

## Method 2 - Prepare a Subject Disposition Data Set
 In eCRF designs, the capture of subject disposition data typically occurs on multiple eCRF pages, and its corresponding EDC system outputs the data in multiple datasets. You can create a derived Subject Disposition data set by reading the relevant fields from EDC system extracts and creating a single dataset with all subject disposition data.

## Prepare Adverse Events Data
 You can extract clinical data for adverse events through the source EDC system. Many EDC systems have the native ability to create extracts of clinical data for adverse events. However, many EDC systems do not have the native ability to create extracts of operational clinical data. In those cases, please contact your EDC vendor to get those extracts. Once you acquire both clinical data and operation data, please contact a programming resource to merge them into a single data set for loading.
- Prepare Adverse Events data with the following parameters to enable CTMS functionality:

If an eCRF form sees multiple uses in a visit, such as a repeat lab test, increment the FRM_RPT_KEY to indicate each extra instance of data collection for the form, for example: FRM_RPT_KEY = 2.

 | Date

 | Acceptable Values: 
- Y – SDV is required and completed. 
- N – SDV is required, but is incomplete. 
 You can also leave this field empty to denote that SDV is optional or completed.
 Note : SDV data requires a combination of both clinical data and clinical operational data. The Medidata Data Fabric requires details about each eCRF form collected which requires SDV, the visit dates for each eCRF form collected, the SDV completion status, and the SDV completion date. If an eCRF form sees multiple uses in a visit, such as a repeat lab test, then increment the FRM_RPT_KEY to indicate each extra instance of data collection for the form, for example: FRM_RPT_KEY = 2. 
## Identify Method to Organize Subject Visit and Source Data Verification Data for Loading
 Identify a method to organize Subject Visit and SDV data files for loading. You can use one of the following methods: 
- Method 1 – Load individual files: one for Subject Visit data and one SDV data. 
- Method 2 – Load a combined file for Subject Visit and SDV Data. 
- Method 3 – Load Subject Visit data by loading source data sets exported from EDC system and loading a file for SDV data. 
- Method 4 – Load Subject Visit data by loading source data sets exported from EDC system including SDV data. 
## Method 1 - Load Individual Files
 You can load the Subject Visit data file and the SDV data file separately.
- Prepare a Subject Visit file containing details about all subject visits that occurred in the study, such as in the example:

## Method 3 - Load Subject Visit Data from Exported from an EDC System and Load an SDV File Separately
 You can prepare Subject Visit data from source data sets that you export from an EDC system. 
 Clinical data exported from EDC system do not map to CTMS functionality and are therefore optional. Only fields pertaining to the required concepts are necessary to enable CTMS functionality.  
- Prepare the Subject Visit data file by extracting source data from your EDC system and doing one of the following to the extract:
- Load all the clinical data into the system.
 Note : This option has an increased loading time. 
- Exclude the optional clinical data fields to prevent loading clinical data into the system.
 Note : The system produces warning messages for fields skipped during data processing.
- Remove the clinical data fields from the extracted data file.
 Note : Regardless of the option you choose, map the required concept tags in the fields SDM file for each data set. Include the correct Data Set and Visit metadata in the Schedule of Assessments SDM file to reflect the study design when loading source system EDC extracts. 
- Similar to method one, prepare an SDV file containing details about all visits that have forms and fields requiring source data verification, such as in the example:

## Method 4 - Load Exported Data Sets from an EDC System That Include Both Subject Visits Data and SDV Data

- Use your EDC system to export data sets containing Subject Visit data and SDV data. 
## Prepare Queries Data

 Important : Special Rules for Queries Data  
- When loading Queries data, the data file must follow a fixed data structure. Ensure the data file matches the listed field names and field order. 
- The Medidata platform does not provide error or warning logs when records fail to load. If you encounter error when loading Queries data, please contact Medidata Customer Support Team for guidance. 
- The Medidata platform identifies duplicates in the file based on the following key columns: QUERYID, QUERY_ACTION, and QUERY_ACTION_DATETIME. Conversely, the platform does not process Queries data based on the Study Design Metadata's Fields file.

## Next Step
 Load SDM Files

===== PAGE: prepare_study_design_metadata_files.html | Prepare Study Design Metadata Files =====
## Prepare Study Design Metadata Files
 Study Design Metadata (SDM) files follow a fixed format. The files you prepare must exactly match the specified file structures and column names. The required files include: 
- Visits – Lists all planned visits for the study. 
- Data Sets – Lists all data sets you intend to load for a study, regardless of data provider. 
- Schedule of Assessments (SOA) – Specifies which visits you intend to have data set data in the study. 
- Fields – Specifies the data fields to include in each data set. 
## General File Creation Rules
- Metadata files must be cumulative. The uploaded file overwrites the corresponding metadata for the corresponding study. 
- Metadata files can only contain content for a single study. 
- Files must be in the comma-separated value (CSV), UTF-8 format. 
- Header records must be the first record in each metadata file. 
- Header records must be uppercase. 
- Field header values must match the values specified under Column Header for each of the below metadata file specifications. 
- If you use commas used in a field value – for example: Please list all events, issues, and problems – then you must double quotes to escape the additional comma: "Please list all events, issues, and problems". 
- Do not use double quotes for null fields, such as '' — instead, denote null fields by keeping the field empty. 
 Important : For each metadata file, fields marked as Required as according to its file specifications must have data. When preparing the content for the SDM file, the field must not be empty. 
## File Naming Rules
 SDM file names must not contain spaces. Key text in each file's name enables the Medidata Data Fabric to determine the type of metadata content contained in the file and how to process it. 
 Tip : In addition the required text, we recommend including the study's name and the date in the file names. These additions can aid you in file management and file versioning when generating and uploading files.
 SDM File Type
 Required File Name
 | Visits
 | Must contain *visits.csv; for example:
- visits.csv 
- my-study_study_design_visits.csv 
- my-study_sdm_visits.csv 
- my-study_2022Sep30_sdm_visits.csv 
 | Data Sets
 | Must contain *datasets.csv; for example:
- datasets.csv 
- myStudy_study_design_datasets.csv 
- myStudy_sdm_datasets.csv 
- myStudy_20220930_sdm_datasets.csv 
 | Schedule of Assessments (SOA)
 | Must contain *soa.csv; for example:
- soa.csv 
- my-study_study_design_soa.csv 
- my-study_sdm_soa.csv 
- my-study_Sep_30_2022_sdm_soa.csv 
 | Fields
 | Must contain *fields.csv; for example: 
- fields.csv 
- myStudy_study_design_fields.csv 
- my-study_sdm_fields.csv 
- myStudy_30Sept2022_sdm_fields.csv 
## Prepare Visits File
 The Visits SDM file lists all planned visits or visit placeholders for the study. 
- Prepare the Visits file according to the following file structure:

- Uppercased alphanumeric text only. 
- No spaces.

- Underscores can appear in an OID, but not as the first character.

If data does not belong to a planned visit and someone can collect that data anytime during a study – such as Adverse Events – create a placeholder visit name such as ALL_VISITS or LOG. If data belongs to planned visit with an unknown visit name, create a placeholder visit name such as UNSCHEDULED.

## Prepare Schedule of Assessments File
 The Schedule of Assessments (SOA) SDM file specifies which visits – planned visits or visit placeholders – you intend to have data set data in the study. Data loading fails if data collected in a study visit differs from the planned pairs of data sets and visits.

- QUERY_TYPE 
- QUERY_CREATED_DATETIME 
- QUERY_ACTION 
- QUERY_ACTION_TEXT 
- QUERY_ACTION_DATETIME 
 For details for the requirements and expected values pertaining to each of these concepts, see  External EDC Standard Concepts .
 Note : If a Field OID has an associated Concept OID, then the Field OID can differ from the Concept OID. 
 For example, a given FIELD_OID and CONCEPT_OID pair can look like the following:
- FIELD_OID – AE_NAME 
- CONCEPT_OID – AETERM. 
 | Char
 | 50
 | No
 | TRANSFORMATION
 | 
 Enter "DIRECT" when you plan to use a field for two or more concepts for Subject Disposition dates, otherwise leave empty. 
 For example, you can use the VISITDT field for both the SVSTDTC and ENROLLED_DATE concepts. 
 | Char
 | 10
 | No
 | FIELD_LABEL
 | The data set field's description.
 | Char
 | 50
 | Yes
 | KEY_VALUE
 | 
 Indicates if the field uniquely identifies a row in a data set.
 If a field set, set the value to 'Y'. Otherwise, set the value to 'N'.
 Important :
- Required values are not always considered Key Field values. 
- If you set the Key Field value to 'Y', then you must also set the Required value to 'Y'. 
 | Char
 | 1
 | Yes
 | REQUIRED
 | Indicates the field must have a value, that is, it is not nullable. If a field cannot be null, then set the value to 'Y'. Otherwise, set the value to 'N'.
 Text must be lowercase.
 | Char
 | 1
 | Yes
 | DATA_TYPE
 | The field's data type.
 Accepted values, as string literals:
- character 
- integer 
- float 
- date 
 | Char
 | 12
 | Yes
 | LENGTH
 | Maximum length of the field. Required if the field is one of:
- character 
- integer 
- float 
 | Integer
 | N/A
 | Conditional
 | SIG_DIGITS
 | Number of significant digits. Required if the field is a float.
 | Integer
 | N/A
 | Conditional
 | DATETIME_FORMAT
 | 
 Date format. Required if the field is a date.
 Allowed Date Formats :
 Date Formats

 | ddMMMyyyy 
 | 04FEB2020 
 | yyyy/MM/dd 
 | 2004/02/04 
 | yyyy/MM/dd HH:nn:ss

 | 20030204 10:23:50 
 | yyyyMMdd 
 | 20030204 
 | yyyymmdd 
 | 20030204 
 | MM/dd/yy 
 | 02/04/03 
 | MM/dd/yyyy 
 | 02/04/2003 
 | dd/MM/yy 
 | 04/02/03 
 | dd/MM/yyyy 
 | 04/02/2003 
 | MM/dd/yy HH:nn:ss 
 | 02/04/03 10:23:30 
 | dd/MM/yy HH:nn:ss 
 | 04/02/03 10:23:30 
 | yy/MM/dd 
 | 03/02/04, 3/2/4 
 | yy/MM/dd HH:nn:ss 
 | 03/02/04 10:23:30 
 | MM/dd/yyyy HH:nn:ss 
 | 02/04/2003 10:23:30 
 | dd/MM/yyyy HH:nn:ss 
 | 04/02/2003 10:23:30 
 | ddMMMyy 
 | 04Feb03 
 | ddMMyyyy 
 | 04022003 
 | MMddyyyy 
 | 02042003 
 Important : Dates relating to queries only accept the yyyymmdd date format.
 | Char
 | 20
 | Conditional
 | SAS_FORMAT
 | SAS format specifier for the field. For details on SAS formats, see the following SAS Reference .
 Text is case insensitive.
 | Char
 | 25
 | No
 | ITEM_GROUP_TYPE
 | Specifies the data field's item group type. Contains one of the following:
- If a field is a Study Master Data Concept, then "common". 
- If a field contains multiple records per subject per visit, then "repeating". 
- If the field contains one record per subject per visit, then "non_repeating". 
 Text is case insensitive.

## Next Step

===== PAGE: redesigned_import_job_creation_workflow.html | Redesigned Import Job Creation Workflow =====
## Redesigned Import Job Creation Workflow
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor updates the New Import Job page. The page steps you through import job creation, and shows different fields depending on the import job type. Each step includes field validation as well.
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-765703, MCC-703764, MCC-757036, MCC-768039 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Ingestor.
## Advantages
 With the updated New Import Job page, you can focus on one step of the job creation process at a time, minimizing information overload. When you choose a data type, the page updates and shows a process bar that helps you know what other information you need to provide. Each page has its own validation process, so that you can ensure each step has the correct information.
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
## User Impact
 On MEDS Ingestor's Import Jobs page, you can click the  Add Import Job button to open the New Import Job page. 
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
 The updated job creation workflow does not impact previously created import jobs. For new import jobs, you can only create jobs for data types that you have permission to. For example, to create external EDC and CTMS jobs, have the relevant building blocks applied to your role. For details about MEDS Ingestor's updated permissions, see  Updated Building Blocks for MEDS Ingestor .
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: redesigned_import_job_list_page.html | Redesigned Import Job List Page =====
## Redesigned Import Job List Page
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor streamlines the Import Jobs page. Upon entering MEDS Ingestor, the import jobs list appears.  The list includes the following attributes: 
- Job Status – The job's status: Active or Completed . 
- Job Name – The job's name. 
- Study – The job's target study. 
- Data Type – The job's data type. 
- Data Integration | Version – The job's data integration name and version. Only applicable to external EDC jobs. 
- Version Status – Denotes if the job's data integration has a new version available. Only applicable to external EDC jobs. 
- Lock Status – Denotes if the job's data integration is locked or unlocked. Only applicable to external EDC jobs. 
- Environment – The job's target study environment. Only applicable to external EDC jobs. 
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-749428, MCC-743837, MCC-771259, MCC-754109, MCC-778938 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Ingestor.
## Advantages
 With the redesigned Jobs List page, you can see your import jobs in a single paginated list. The list shows the jobs' relevant data without needing to inspect individual jobs. You can filter the list, which allows you to quickly find and manage specific jobs.   
## User Impact
 The Import Jobs page appears when you enter MEDS Ingestor from iMedidata.
 From the Import Jobs page, you can perform the following actions:
- Click  Add Import Job to open the New Import Job wizard. 
- Sort the import jobs list by any of the attributes. 
- Click any of the listed import jobs to open the job's Job Files page. 
- Click any job's gear icon to open the job's Job Configurations page. 
 The updated import job list does not affect your import job visibility or access. If you cannot see or access your previously created import jobs, review  Updated Building Blocks for MEDS Ingestor and ensure that your role has the appropriate building blocks.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: redesigned_import_summary_panel.html | Redesigned Import Summary Panel =====
## Redesigned Import Summary Panel
## Description
 MEDS Ingestor updates the Import Summary panel. The Import Summary panel shows a summary of every file uploaded during the data import process in one view. For each file, the page also shows the file's details, processing summary, and any downloadable artifacts related to the import.
 Tip : Click image to enlarge.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-751376 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to MEDS Ingestor.
## Advantages
 With the redesigned Import Summary screen panel, you can view  details of multiple imported files in a streamlined view. From this view, you can see each file's respective processing summary, and download any related import summary artifacts. This saves you the effort of reviewing multiple files across separate screens.  
## User Impact
 You can access the Import Summary panel from the Jobs List page by clicking the Import Summary link for any failed or successful uploaded file.
 This update does not affect your import file visibility or access. If you cannot see or access your previously created import jobs, review  Updated Building Blocks for MEDS Ingestor and ensure that your role has the appropriate building blocks.
## Special Considerations
 None.

===== PAGE: review_job_configuration_details.html | Review Job Configuration Details =====
## Review Job Configuration Details
 After creating one or more import jobs , you can use Ingestor to review and manage job details on each job's Job Configuration screen. The presented details differ depending on the type of import job.  
## Open Job Configuration Page
- Log into iMedidata.com . 
- Navigate to Ingestor through an assigned client division. 
- Select an import job to review.
 Tip : You can filter import jobs by different parameters, such as Study, Job Type, and Environment. To filter the import job list, click the Filter icon  for one of the parameters, and select which data to filter by.
- Click the Job Configuration tab.
 Tip : You can also access the Job Configuration page by clicking the gear icon from the jobs list.
## Job Details
 Job Name 
 Import job's name and Job ID.
 Tip : You can click the clipboard icon to copy the Job ID to your clipboard. You can use the Job ID when using the Medidata API or with Medidata Support. Click image to enlarge.
 Study 
 Name of the data import's target study.
 Study Environment 
 Name of the data import's target study environment.
 Note : This field is only applicable for Study Design Metadata jobs and external EDC  jobs.
 Data Type 
 Type of data the job imports.
 Created 
 The user who created this job, and when. This field is in the following format: dd-mmm-yyyy hh:mm:ss. Time is in UTC. 
 Last Modified 
 The user who last edited the import job, and when. This field is in the following format: dd-mmm-yyyy hh:mm:ss. Time is in UTC. 
## Data Integration Details
 Note : Data integration details are only available when reviewing external EDC jobs.
 Data Integration Name 
 The data integration's name. 
 Data Integration Version 
 The data integration  version. 
 Data Integration Version Creation Date 
 The data integration  version's creation date  in the following format: dd-mmm-yyyy. 
 Data Integration Published Date 
 The data integration  version's creation date  in the following format: dd-mmm-yyyy. 
 Source Protocol Configuration Name 
 The source protocol configuration name. 
 Source Protocol Configuration Version 
 The source protocol configuration version number. 
 Source Protocol Configuration Creation Date 
 The source protocol configuration creation date in the following format: dd-mmm-yyyy. 
 Source Protocol Configuration Published Date 
 The source protocol configuration publication date in the following format: dd-mmm-yyyy. 
## Import Settings
 Import Type 
 Denotes whether the processing job is Cumulative, replacing all existing data; or Incremental, adding to or updating existing data. Processing jobs with the incremental import type can Upsert – add or update existing items – or Delete items. The uploaded file's name determines if Ingestor upserts or deletes items. If the file's name contains "upsert", then upsertion occurs. If the file's name contains "delete", then deletion occurs. 
 Note :
- An uploaded file can only include "upsert" or "delete", and not both. 
- This field is only available for external EDC jobs 
 Source 
 The indicates where the uploaded data comes from : 
- SFTP   – Indicates that the job uses SFTP import. 
- Not Available   – Indicates that the job uses manual import. 
 SFTP Location 
 The name of the SFTP location used by the import job.
 Folder Location to Retrieve Files 
 The extension of the path in the SFTP location where Ingestor imports data files from .
 Folder Location for Output Status Files 
 The extension of the path in the SFTP location where Ingestor saves process files .
 Run Job Now 
 For SFTP-enabled jobs, you can click SFTP Input Folder Now to force an SFTP exchange. The import job then runs in the same manner as a regularly scheduled SFTP job run.

===== PAGE: review_job_notifications.html | Review Job Notifications =====
## Review Job Notifications
 After creating one or more import jobs  with configured SFTP settings , you can view notifications about any import jobs that use SFTP locations from a job's Job Notifications page. The Job Notifications page shows any errors and warnings that occurred while the import job tried accessing the SFTP location or file path. A notification icon appears when there are new notifications available. Notifications refresh every 30 minutes. 
## Open Job Configuration Page
- Log into iMedidata.com . 
- Navigate to Ingestor through an assigned client division. 
- Select an import job to review.
 Tip : You can filter import jobs by different parameters, such as Study, Job Type, and Environment. To filter the import job list, click the Filter icon  for one of the parameters, and select which data to filter by.
- Click the Job Notifications tab.
 Tip : You can also access the Job Notifications page by clicking the alert icon from the jobs list.
## Notification Details
 Type 
 Description – The notification's type. Possible values: 
- Error 
- Warning 
 Message 
 Description –  The notification's message, including the subject line and details. 
 Category 
 Description –  The category the notification pertains to. Possible values: 
- STFP 
- FTPS 
 Last Occurrence Date 
 Description – The date and time the error or warning last occurred .
 Number of Occurrences 
 Description –  The number of times that the error or warning occurred. 
 Tip : You can dismiss notifications by clicking Dismiss  icon.

===== PAGE: run_now_button_available_for_sftp-enabled_import_jobs.html | Run Now Button Available for SFTP-Enabled Import Jobs =====
## Run Now Button Available for SFTP-Enabled Import Jobs
## Description
 For SFTP-enabled import jobs, you can force an SFTP exchange from the Job Configuration Screen.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-811204 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the ability to see and use the Job Configuration screen. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 Every 30 minutes, MEDS Ingestor imports the files from the Input File Path and exports various status files to the output folder path. With this update, you can test the SFTP configuration settings and confirm successful imports before going live with production data without needing to wait for the first scheduled SFTP run.
## User Impact
 From an import job's Job Configuration screen, you force an SFTP exchange by clicking  Check SFTP Input Folder Now . The import job then runs in the same manner as a regularly scheduled SFTP job run.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support this enhancement: 
- Review Job Configuration Details 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal . 
## Description
 For SFTP-enabled import jobs, you can force an SFTP exchange from the Job Configuration Screen.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-811204 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with the ability to see and use the Job Configuration screen. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 Every 30 minutes, MEDS Ingestor imports the files from the Input File Path and exports various status files to the output folder path. With this update, you can test the SFTP configuration settings and confirm successful imports before going live with production data without needing to wait for the first scheduled.
## User Impact
 From an import job's Job Configuration screen, you force an SFTP exchange by clicking  Check SFTP Input Folder Now . The import job then runs in the same manner as a regularly scheduled SFTP job run.
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support this enhancement: 
- Review Job Configuration Details 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: sas_file_support_for_external_edc_jobs.html | SAS File Support for External EDC Jobs =====
## SAS File Support for External EDC Jobs
## Description
 You can now use SAS (*.xpt) files to import clinical data onto the Medidata Platform with External EDC import jobs. You can upload SAS files with the file import interface or with SFTP. MEDS Ingestor only supports the SAS V5 Transport file format and below.
 Feature/enhancement stories referenced in this article:
 MCC-801498, MCC-810509, MCC-849447 
## Affected Roles
 All roles with access to import data with External EDC jobs. For details, see  Ingestor Roles and Permissions List .
## Advantages
 You can have more options to upload your clinical data, saving you possible issues when converting from the SAS format to the comma-separated value (*.csv) format.
## User Impact
 When uploading files to External EDC import jobs, you can use SAS files in the .XPT format. The Job Configuration screen for external EDC jobs also lists the new file type.
 Note :
- Standard Medidata Concepts formatting rules still apply when using SAS files. For details, see  External EDC Standard Concepts . 
- Uploaded SAS files must be version 5 or lower. 
## Knowledge Space Updates
 The MEDS Ingestor Knowledge Space provides updated content to support this enhancement: 
- Quick Start Ingestor - Import Clinical Data from External EDC Sources 
- Import Data 
- Import Clinical Data from External EDC Sources 
## Special Considerations
 To view Medidata testing documentation for this release, refer to the Medidata Validation Portal .

===== PAGE: sftp_configuration_for_import_jobs_-_workflow.html | SFTP Configuration for Import Jobs - Workflow =====
## SFTP Configuration for Import Jobs - Workflow
 With Ingestor, you can configure import jobs to download data from an SFTP location. The workflow below outlines the process pertaining to configuring and using SFTP locations in Ingestor. 
 MEDSINGEST_Workflow_Master_SFTP-Config_ENG
- Using Extractor, add an SFTP location to your client division. 
- Use Ingestor create an import job . During the creation process, configure the job to use the SFTP location.
 Note : If you choose not to configure a job's SFTP settings during its creation, or want to edit an existing job's SFTP settings, you can do so from the job's configuration screen .
- You can use Ingestor to review any updates pertaining to the SFTP location. 
- Every 30 minutes, Ingestor imports the files from the SFTP locations' Input File Path and exports various status files to the output folder path. When applicable, the exported files include processing files such as error lists, reconciliation reports, and success logs.

===== PAGE: study_design_metadata_file_standards.html | Study Design Metadata File Standards =====
## Study Design Metadata File Standards
 Study Design Metadata (SDM) files follow a fixed format. The files you prepare must exactly match the specified file structures and column names. The required files include: 
- Visits – Lists all planned visits for the study. 
- Data Sets – Lists all data sets you intend to load for a study, regardless of data provider. 
- Schedule of Assessments (SOA) – Specifies which visits you intend to have data set data in the study. 
- Fields – Specifies the data fields to include in each data set. 
 For details about loading SDM data for CTMS, see  Import Non-Rave Data for Medidata CTMS .
## General File Creation Rules
- Metadata files must be cumulative. The uploaded file overwrites the corresponding metadata for the corresponding study. 
- Metadata files can only contain content for a single study. 
- Files must be in the comma-separated value (CSV), UTF-8 format. 
- Header records must be the first record in each metadata file. 
- Header records must be uppercase. 
- Field header values must match the values specified under Column Header for each of the below metadata file specifications. 
- If you use commas used in a field value – for example: Please list all events, issues, and problems – then you must double quotes to escape the additional comma: "Please list all events, issues, and problems". 
- Do not use double quotes for null fields, such as '' — instead, denote null fields by keeping the field empty. 
 Important : For each metadata file, fields marked as Required as according to its file specifications must have data. When preparing the content for the SDM file, the field must not be empty. 
## Standard SDM File Names
 The names of the four SDM files are standardized and must be as follows:
 SDM File Type
 Required File Name
 | Visits
 | Must contain *visits.csv; for example:
- visits.csv 
- my-study_study_design_visits.csv 
- my-study_sdm_visits.csv 
- my-study_2022Sep30_sdm_visits.csv 
 | Data Sets
 | Must contain *datasets.csv; for example:
- datasets.csv 
- myStudy_study_design_datasets.csv 
- myStudy_sdm_datasets.csv 
- myStudy_20220930_sdm_datasets.csv 
 | Schedule of Assessments (SOA)
 | Must contain *soa.csv; for example:
- soa.csv 
- my-study_study_design_soa.csv 
- my-study_sdm_soa.csv 
- my-study_Sep_30_2022_sdm_soa.csv 
 | Fields
 | Must contain *fields.csv; for example: 
- fields.csv 
- myStudy_study_design_fields.csv 
- my-study_sdm_fields.csv 
- myStudy_30Sept2022_sdm_fields.csv 
## Study Design Metadata File Formats
 SDM files must follow a specific file structure. Your files must include the following required columns, and may include the following optional columns:
## Visits
 The Visits SDM file lists all planned visits or visit placeholders for the study.

 Important : OID Requirements 
- Uppercased alphanumeric text only. 
- No spaces. 
- The OID must begin with an alphabetical character. 
- Underscores can appear in an OID, but not as the first character.

 Note : You must associate all data you intend to load with a planned visit, such as Screening. If data does not belong to a planned visit and someone can collect that data anytime during a study – such as Adverse Events – create a placeholder visit name such as ALL_VISITS or LOG. If data belongs to planned visit with an unknown visit name, create a placeholder visit name such as UNSCHEDULED.

## Schedule of Assessments
 The Schedule of Assessments (SOA) SDM file specifies which visits – planned visits or visit placeholders – you intend to have data set data in the study. Data loading fails if data collected in a study visit differs from the planned pairs of data sets and visits.

## Fields
 The fields SDM file specifies the data fields to include in each data set. The content in the fields files also provides key information to the system to process the data.
 Important : Any data pertaining to the Queries data file must follow a strict order for the sequence of rows:
- QUERYID 
- STUDYID 
- SITEID 
- SUBJID 
- VISIT 
- SVSTDTC 
- FRM_NAME 
- FRM_RPT_KEY 
- FIELD_NAME 
- QUERY 
- QUERY_OPEN_METHOD 
- QUERY_TYPE 
- QUERY_CREATED_DATETIME 
- QUERY_ACTION 
- QUERY_ACTION_TEXT 
- QUERY_ACTION_DATETIME 
 Column Heading
 Description / Content Expectations
 Data Type
 Length
 Required?
 | STUDY_NAME
 | “Study Name” field value as it appears in Cloud Administration, for example: MY_STUDY.
 | Char
 | 256
 | Yes
 | DATASET_OID
 | OID of a data set for upload. The DATASET_OID must exist in the Data Sets SDM file and Schedule of Assessments SDM file.
 | Char
 | 50
 | Yes
 | FIELD_ORDER
 | 
 The sequential order of the fields in a data set, for example: '1', '2', '3', etc. This sorts the data in the Fields file in ascending order.
 Note : FIELD_ORDER does not need to match the column position in the data file except in the Queries data set. The Queries data set follows a strict column order. 
 | Integer
 | N/A
 | Yes
 | FIELD_OID
 | 
 The OID for the field — the field's short name. Examples include: 
- AE_NAME 
- QUERYID 
- VISIT_DATE 
 Important : OID Requirements 
- Uppercased alphanumeric text only. 
- No spaces. 
- The OID must begin with an alphabetical character. 
- Underscores can appear in an OID, but not as the first character. 
 Note : If a Field OID has an associated Concept OID, then the Field OID can differ from the Concept OID.
 | Char
 | 50
 | Yes
 | CONCEPT_OID
 | 
 Provides semantic meaning for a Field OID, for example:
- AE_NAME 
- SVSTDTC 
- SCREENING_DATE 
 Important : You must use a valid Medidata Standard Concept OID when populating the Concept OID. Each data set has required concepts you must include.
 For details for the requirements and expected values pertaining to each of these concepts, see  External EDC Standard Concepts .
 Note : If a Field OID has an associated Concept OID, then the Field OID can differ from the Concept OID. 
 For example, a given FIELD_OID and CONCEPT_OID pair can look like the following:
- FIELD_OID – AE_NAME 
- CONCEPT_OID – AETERM. 
 | Char
 | 50
 | No
 | TRANSFORMATION

===== PAGE: study_design_metadata_process.html | Study Design Metadata Import Process =====
## Study Design Metadata Import Process
 You can use Study Design Metadata (SDM) files to automate the creation of data integrations and protocol configurations. You can then use the data integrations to import external data into the Medidata platform. Importing SDM files creates both of :
- A protocol configuration named <study name>Protocol Configuration at version 1. 
- A data integration named External EDC Data Integration at version 1. 
 For details regarding the composition of SDM files, see  Study Design Metadata Files . 
 Note :  Subsequent successful imports of updated SDM files increment the version number. 
## Study Design Metadata Import Workflow
 Ingestor processes SDM files using the following numbered steps shown in the schematic:
- You create a Ingestor import job with the Study Design Metadata data type and import the four SDM files simultaneously. SDM files are standard CSV files that describe the format of the third-party data that an import job ingests. 
- Designer Loader, a Ingestor component, generates  a new Data Integration for the study from the four SDM files. 
- You use the  Import Data workflow to specify the generated data integration and import third-party data files. 
- Ingestor checks if  the third-party data file matches the assigned data integration configuration. 
- Ingestor imports your data into the Medidata platform. 
 SDM Managed Importing

===== PAGE: study_master_data_file_standards.html | Study Master Data File Standards =====
## Study Master Data File Standards
 To ensure the successful import of Study Level Master Data files to Ingestor, the files must follow specific file name and format conventions. The imported data corresponds to various Cloud Administration fields, and you can confirm the data changes in Cloud Administration.
## General File Considerations
- Uploaded files must be in the comma-separated value (CSV) file format.
- Files containing empty data cause the import process to fail. 
- Any OID identifier referenced in these files must comply with the following rules: 
- Uppercase alphanumeric text only. 
- No spaces. 
- The OID must begin with an alphabetical character. 
- Underscores can appear in an OID, but not as the first character. 
## File Name Standards
 The file names for your imported files must contain a Char denoting which data set  the imported file corresponds to.
 Tip : For files you can use as a model and starting point, see  Starter Import Files . 
 Data Set Type 
 Usage 
 Required Char in File Name 
 | Client Division Organization with Addresses 
 | 
 Create and add multiple client division organizations (CDO) to your client division.
 | CDOwithAddresses 
 | Client Division People with Addresses 
 | 
 Create and add multiple people to your client division.
 | CDPWithAddresses 
 | Study Environment Sites with Addresses 
 | 
 Create and add multiple study environment sites to your client division.
 | StudyEnvironmentSiteWithAddresses 
 | Team Membership Details 
 | 
 Create or update team membership for  client division people at the study environment level.
 | TeamMembershipDetails 
 | Team Member Contact Details 
 | 
 Create or update team member contact information for your client division.
 | TeamMemberContactDetails 
 Note : The required Char is case-insensitive, and you can prefix or append alphanumeric characters to the Char by separating the added text with underscores. For example, when uploading a file corresponding to the Client Division Organization with Addresses data set type, the file name must contain "CDOwithAddresses". Therefore, Ingestor accepts or rejects the following example file names:
- Valid File Names
- CDOwithAddresses_20210604.csv 
- Cdowithadddresses.csv 
- SponsorA_cdoWithAddresses.csv 
- Invalid File Names 
- CODwidthaddreses.csv
- CDwithaddresses.csv
## File Structure Standards
 Uploaded files must follow a specific file structure:
- Your files must include all listed column headers, but optional columns can include empty data. 
- Header records must be on the first row of each metadata file, and must the values specified in the below table's Column Header. 
- Headers with noted acceptance values must contain one or more of the listed values.   
- If a Char-based value contains a comma in the Char, you must enclose the entire value in quotations. For example: “Congo, Democratic Republic of“. 
 Tip : Click image to expand.
## Client Division Organizations with Addresses
 Note :  When importing files containing Client Division Organization with Addresses data, you can only create a client division organization with one Main address.

 | Email 
 | The person's email address. 
 | Required 
 | String
 | N/A 
 | 
 Must be an alphanumeric email address. If not, Ingestor shows the following error message: "Email is in invalid format."
 Note : When creating a client division person, Ingestor sets the person's Email Type to "Work", and sets the email as the person's primary email.
 | Title (optional) 
 | The person's title. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Must be one of the following:
- Mr. 
- Mrs. 
- Ms. 
- Dr. 
- Prof. 
 | First Name 
 | The person's first name. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | 255 
 | None. 
 | Middle Name (optional) 
 | The person's middle name. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | 255 
 | None. 
 | Last Name 
 | The person's surname. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | None. 
 | Type 
 | The person's type. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Must be one of the following:
- Internal 
- External 
 | Person ID (optional) 
 | The person's user-entered unique identifier. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | 36 
 | Must be unique within the client division.
 | Job Title (optional) 
 | The person's job title. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | 100 
 | None. 
 | Organizations (optional) 
 | A list of the person's client division organizations. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | You can provide multiple values by separating each value with a pipe delimiter in the CSV, for example: Medi University|Southeast Medical Facility. 
 | Is Phone Primary 
 | Denotes if the value in the Phone field is the person's primary phone number. 
 | Conditional 
 | True/False String 
 | N/A 
 | 
 Must be one of the following:
- True 
- False 
 Required if you entered a value in the Phone field.
 If this field corresponds to a new person record, then both the Is Phone Primary field must be "True" and the Phone field must have data.
 | Phone (optional) 
 | The persona's phone number. You can add phone numbers or update existing phone numbers. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | 
 There are no expected values for this field. 
 Required when at least one of the following conditions is true:
- You entered a value in the Phone Type field. 
- You entered "True" in the Is Phone Primary field. 
 If this field corresponds to a new person record, then both the Is Phone Primary field must be "True" and the Phone field must have data.
 | Phone Type 
 | The person's phone number type. 
 | Conditional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Must be one of the following:
- Work 
- Home 
- Mobile 
- Fax 
- Other 
 Required if you entered a value in the Phone field.
 | Is Email Primary 
 | Denotes if the value in the Email field is the person's primary phone number. 
 | Conditional 
 | String
 | N/A 
 | Must be one of the following:
- True 
- False 
 Required if you entered a value in the Email field.
 | Email (optional) 
 | The person's phone number. You can add email addresses or update existing email address. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | 
 Must be a valid alphanumeric email address.
 Required when at least one of the following conditions is true:
- You entered a value in the Email Type field. 
- You entered "True" in the Is Email Primary field. 
 | Email Type 
 | The person's email address type. 
 | Conditional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Must be one of the following:
- Work 
- Personal 
- Other 
 Required if you entered a value in the Email field.
 | Address Types 
 | The person's physical address type.
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | 
 Must be one of the following:
- Main 
- Office 
- Personal/Home 
 You can provide multiple values by separating each value with a pipe delimiter in the CSV, for example: address1@domain.com|address2@domain.com 
 If this field corresponds to the first row of a new person record, then you must enter "Main".
 | Country 
 | The country of the person's physical address. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | None. This is a free text field.  
 | Address 1 
 | The first field of the person's physical address. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | None. This is a free text field.  
 | Address 2 (optional) 
 | The second field of the person's physical address. 
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | None. This is a free text field.  
 | City 
 | The city of the person's physical address. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | None. This is a free text field.  
 | State/Region 
 | The country's subregion of the person's physical address.  
 | Conditional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Must be a valid subregion for the country. 
 Required if the Country is one of the following:
- Australia 
- Canada 
- China 
- Japan 
- Russian Federation 
- South Korea 
- United States 
 | Postal Code 
 | The postal code of the person's physical address. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | None. This is a free text field.

 Attribute Name 
 Attribute Description 
 Attribute Type 
 Data Type 
 Length 
 Content Expectations / Validations 
 | Study Name 
 | The associated study name as it appears in Cloud Administration. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | When creating a site record, this field must match an existing study within the client division.
 When updating a site record, you cannot use this field to edit the study name.
 | Site Name 
 | The associated site name as it appears in Cloud Administration. 
 | Conditional 
 | String 
 | 255 
 | Only required when there is no existing client division organization(CDO) record associated with the site. Ingestor ignores this field for sites with existing CDO records. When Ingestor processes the CDO, the site name serves as both the site name and legal name for the created CDO.
 When creating a CDO record, this field must match an existing study within the client division.
 When updating a CDO record, you cannot use this field to edit the site name.
 | Client Division Organization ID 
 | The client division organization’s unique identification number (CDO ID). 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | 255 
 | Only required when there is no existing client division organization (CDO) record associated with the site. Ingestor ignores this field for existing CDOs. When Ingestor processes the CDO, the site name serves as both the site name and legal name for the created CDO.
 When creating a CDO record:
- If this entry matches an existing CDO ID, then Ingestor:
- Applies the CDO's Legal Name to the Site Name. 
- Uses the CDO's Main Address as the site's Mail Address. 
- If this entry does not match an existing CDO ID record, then Ingestor creates a CDO with a new Site while using the COD's Legal Name as the Site Name and links the CDO ID to the new site. 
 When updating a CDO record, you cannot use this field to edit the COD ID.
 | Study Site Number
 | The unique number of the study environment site. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Ingestor ignores this field for existing site records. 
 | Study Environment Name
 | 
 The associated study environment name as it appears in Cloud Administration. 
 | Required 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | 
 Must be one of the following:
- Production 
- Development 
- Functional Testing 
- Interactive Verification 
- User Acceptance Testing 
- Training 
 Note : Currently, Ingestor only supports standard, non-custom study environments.  
 | Country 
 | The country of the study site's address.
 | Conditional
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Only required under at least one of the following conditions:
- There is no existing client division organization (CDO) associated with the site. 
- You entered data in one of the other address fields. 
 Ingestor ignores this field for existing CDOs.
 When creating a CDO record:
- Ingestor s ets the CDO's Main address as the associated Site's Main address. 
- If the address in this field does not correspond to a Main address, then Ingestor considers the address as an alternative address. Alternative addresses must have an Address Type other than Main, and must be unique compared to the Main address. 
- If this address corresponds to Main address, then the address must exactly match an existing Main address as it appears in the Medidata platform. Failure to do so results in the error: "Main address does not match CDO Main address. 
- If the Site address exactly matches any of the parent CDO's alternative addresses, then Ingestor links the address between the CDO and the site, and adds the address to the CDO. 
 When updating a CDO record: 
- You can add addresses. 
- You can also add or remove addresses' address types. Address edits are unique to the site – the CDO does not change. You cannot change the Main address from Ingestor. 
 | Address 1 
 | The first field of the study site's address.
 | Conditional
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Only required under at least one of the following conditions:
- There is no existing client division organization (CDO) associated with the site. 
- You entered data in one of the other address fields. 
 Ingestor ignores this field for existing CDOs.
 When creating a CDO record:
- Ingestor s ets the CDO's Main address as the associated Site's Main address. 
- If the address in this field does not correspond to a Main address, then Ingestor considers the address as an alternative address. Alternative addresses must have an Address Type other than Main, and must be unique compared to the Main address. 
- If this address corresponds to Main address, then the address must exactly match an existing Main address as it appears in the Medidata platform. Failure to do so results in the error: "Main address does not match CDO Main address. 
- If the Site address exactly matches any of the parent CDO's alternative addresses, then Ingestor links the address between the CDO and the site, and adds the address to the CDO. 
 When updating a CDO record: 
- You can add addresses. 
- You can also add or remove addresses' address types. Address edits are unique to the site – the CDO does not change. You cannot change the Main address from Ingestor. 
 | Address 2 (optional) 
 | The second field of the study site's address.
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Only required under at least one of the following conditions:
- There is no existing client division organization (CDO) associated with the site. 
- You entered data in one of the other address fields. 
 Ingestor ignores this field for existing CDOs.

- If the address in this field does not correspond to a Main address, then Ingestor considers the address as an alternative address. Alternative addresses must have an Address Type other than Main, and must be unique compared to the Main address. 
- If this address corresponds to Main address, then the address must exactly match an existing Main address as it appears in the Medidata platform. Failure to do so results in the error: "Main address does not match CDO Main address. 
- If the Site address exactly matches any of the parent CDO's alternative addresses, then Ingestor links the address between the CDO and the site, and adds the address to the CDO.

 | Conditional

 | Postal Code 
 | The postal code of the study site's address.
 | Conditional

 | Address Types 
 | The study site address's type.
 | Conditional
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Only required under at least one of the following conditions:
- There is no existing client division organization (CDO) associated with the site. 
- You entered data in one of the other address fields. 
 Ingestor ignores this field for existing CDOs.
 Must be one of the following:
- Main 
- Drug/Device Shipping 
- Finance Billing 
- General Shipping Location 
- Laboratory 
- Laboratory Supply Location 
- Other 
- PI Location 
- Pharmacy 
- Satellite Site 
- Study Coordinator Location 
- Sub Investigator Location 
 When creating a site record:
- If the site's address exactly matches the parent CDO's Main address, then the site address's type must be Main. 
-  If the site's address does not exactly match the parent CDO's Main address, then the site address's type must be a type other than Main. 
- If the site address's type does not exist in the parent CDO's records, then Ingestor sets the corresponding parent CDO's site address type to "Other".  
 When updating a site record, you can add or remove one or more non-Main  addresses. However, the changes to the site record do not sync with the CDO record.
 | PI Email Address (optional) 
 | 
 The email address of the  Prinicipal Investigator (PI) associated with the study site.
 | Optional 
 | String 
 | N/A 
 | Must be a valid alphanumeric email address linked to an existing Cloud Administration user.
